Nothing failed and nothing was rerun. The targeted break of the fixtures guard was not made (no further mutation campaign was asked for), so the two new tests are known to pass and are not known to fail when the guard is removed.

**Not run, and not claimed:** `pnpm run test:browser` (nothing in the Ops Portal changed); `pnpm audit` (no dependency changed; the last run is recorded under S0.10); every Linux, WSL2 and CI run (OB-62), including the tests that Windows skips, which stay **pending**; the hosted run of the pipeline; the B-05 items the owner holds, which stay **pending**; the release obligations (production provisioning access among them), which stay **pending**; `db:migrate` and `db:seed` against `melarc_dev` (OB-82); any behaviour of an identity operation. **Timing failures, cause unresolved.** During development `execution-budget.db.test.ts`, whose tests depend on the server's own timers, failed once inside a whole-project run and once as a file, a different test each time; other work was loading this workstation at the time, which is an observation and not an established cause. It passed alone eight times (the idle-in-transaction test), six times as a file, in the gate of 10 October and in the run on the corrected tree (a pass is not a diagnosis). These are intermittent failures observed under load: no root cause has been shown, no timeout was raised and no suite was repeated to obtain green. The cause stays open (see the limitations).

**Decisions of the review (10 October 2026).** Rejecting an arm for an unknown principal type: kept, it fits the closed security-context model. Requiring a non-empty array for all seven seed tables: accepted for this reference seed, every current table has required content. The seed content as JSON: accepted for this gate with the trust boundary stated below. Production fixture protection: `FIXTURE_` is a naming restriction, the limitation is explicit below and production provisioning access is settled before release. The unknown-commit HTTP mapping: due before S0.20 (OB-55). The orphan databases: untouched. Expressionless policies (a policy with no `USING` or no `WITH CHECK`): investigated and ruled out as a bypass, PostgreSQL default-denies when no permissive expression grants access; no repair is requested.

**Limitations that remain, and who owns each.**

| Limitation| Owner and milestone|
|---|---|
| **Trust boundary of the seed.** The authorized provisioner chooses the initial content (the JSON document); the database enforces the rules of an initialization (once, into empty tables, atomically, serialized, at revision 1) and **does not verify that the content is the approved catalogue**, which the seed data's Family Z tests and the owner's review hold to its documents. Whether the first content must also be fixed in a migration is decided with the controlled primitive| S0.27 (bundle-change primitive)|
| **`FIXTURE_` is a naming restriction, not an environment restriction.** `melarc.add_reason_fixtures` stays callable with the provisioning credentials wherever they are held, so a production database can receive fixture reasons from anyone who holds them; the production refusal is the command's only. Who holds the provisioning credentials in production, and whether the function exists there, is settled before release| The Product Owner, before release (deployment and release gates)|
| **Orphan test databases.** Four `melarc_test_*` databases with no session remain on the local server (`db:orphans` lists them, nothing was dropped); ownership is not established and cleanup is not authorized| The owner (leave untouched until ownership is established and cleanup is authorized)|
| The unknown-commit outcome has no HTTP mapping yet| The Product Owner, then S0.20 (OB-55)|
| The policy reader accepts two structures; a form a later table needs (the policy template of the first hub-scoped table) is added to the reader with its own safety test| The first slice with a hub-scoped table|
| The readiness probe sends no server-side cancel when its deadline passes; its connection is destroyed and the server's statement timeout bounds the query (a catalogue lookup)| Accepted; revisit with S0.65|
| Settings needed and not settled: the retention periods per category (`OQ-028`), first read by S0.19; the notification and handshake keys (settings.md §7.4), first read by S0.17 and S0.18; the evidence limits and the accounting-export retention are not used by this slice| The Product Owner, at the named task|
| A null tier is unproved against a tier-restricted action until a task compares tiers| S0.27 (OB-75)|
| The workflow reasons are seeded by the slices that use them| Those slices (OB-83)|
| `melarc_dev` has not had migrations 0003 to 0005 or the seed applied| The owner (OB-82)|
| The timeout, restart and cancel figures are development values| S0.65 (benchmark and capacity)|
| Intermittent failures of the timer-based tests in `execution-budget.db.test.ts`, observed twice under load; **the cause is unresolved** (no timeout raised, no repeat-until-green)| The owner; investigate before those tests are relied on as a gate, S0.65 at the latest (OB-91)|

### 7.13 Carried from S0.11 to S0.16 (batch of 10 October 2026)

The owner's instruction of 10 October 2026 authorized the Stage 1 checkpoint commit and then S0.11 to S0.16 as one bounded batch, left uncommitted and unpushed for review. Stage 2 therefore has its first six tasks built. **Gate 2 is not passed and is not claimed:** S0.17 to S0.19 were outside the batch (they are [§7.14](#714-carried-from-s017-to-s019-batch-of-10-october-2026)'s), and Gate 2 needs the worker's real `SIGTERM` shutdown test run on Linux, WSL2 or CI (OB-24). The evidence is *fixture/platform verified* (a real PostgreSQL 18.6, the technical logins, the kit); no operation of the contract exists, so none of it is *real-operation* evidence. The rows below are what the batch decided on its own authority or found in the documents and did not settle; none is a default for a product value, and none changes an identifier, the OpenAPI document, a generated client or a contract.

| ID| Kind| Obligation or finding| Source| Built and proved by| Run| State|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OB-93| Finding| **The audit catalogue and its own grammar disagree.** audit.md §4 says an event's domain is one of the seventeen of permissions.md §4; the §5 catalogue holds 26 `auth` and 6 `order` codes, and neither domain is on that list. The document also states 188 codes where its tables hold 190. The writer honours the catalogue (`audit-event-codes.ts`, compared with the tables by a test) and adds no DB check on the domain. The owner decides whether the grammar is widened or the codes renamed| audit.md §4, §5| The Product Owner| M| Open|
| OB-94| Finding| **"Enhanced requires `before_after`" and a hub-boundary read contradict.** audit.md §6 says each enhanced event "requires `before_after` populated"; §5.4 says `auth.hub_boundary.crossed` (enhanced) "changes nothing, so `before_after` stays empty". Neither the writer nor the table enforces the first; the owner decides which stands| audit.md §5.4, §6| The Product Owner| M| Open|

Authenticated-API traffic (the 120-a-window bucket) can therefore fill the table and refuse sign-in, MFA and recovery for everybody until rows leave the window; §14.12 answers with "bounded local admission and the edge's per-source ceiling" before a row is written. The document does not say whether a bucket should reserve room. **Recommendation:** reserve a share of the cap for the credential buckets, or count the authenticated-API bucket in memory per process at the edge; the owner decides, and `MAX_TOTAL_ROWS` is a development-qualified figure either way (OB-106)| review of this batch; SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.12| The Product Owner, before the first exposure of authentication; S0.65| D| Open|

**Review of the batch (10 October 2026) and what it changed.** A read-only reviewer read the whole batch against the registry, the contract and the design (it could run nothing). Nothing it reported was taken on trust: each finding was checked against the code and, where it held, fixed test first, the new test being seen to fail for the stated reason before the change. Eight findings, none critical or high. (1) *Medium, real:* an event the audit writer itself refuses fails before any statement is sent, so the transaction stayed healthy and a caller that caught the refusal committed the act with no record; the writer now aborts the transaction (three cases). (2) *Medium, real:* the limiter's cleanup made admissions for different keys queue on each other's deletes, against its own doc comment; one admission at a time now cleans, by a try-lock (OB-109), shown with a test that failed by timeout and three breaks. (3) *Low, real:* the `authentication` arms of `idempotency_record` were wider than the registry's "erasing the sealed columns only" (read every saved answer, complete any command in progress); the policies now admit it to records that hold or held a sealed replay and the update must erase it (the check clause of the update policy is defence in depth that no test can tell from the guard trigger, an equivalent mutant; the other two clauses are each caught by a test that does not touch the first). (4) *Low, real, not reachable yet:* the audit correlation id is now screened like any text. (5) *Low, real:* a `SYSTEM` transaction can no longer name its own technical identifier as a person. (6) *Low, real:* the handle's context is now the one the database service applied (a regression test over the real service caught a shadowed variable in the change itself; the service also now judges the capability on its frozen copy, not on the caller's object). (7) *Low, real:* a diagnostic sink that throws no longer fails production counting. (8) *Low, real:* the test that claimed different keys do not serialise could not detect one global lock; it is replaced by one that proves both key locks are held at the same moment (a break with one constant key lock is caught). Of the four items the reviewer could not verify: the shared capacity is OB-110, `recorded_at` is OB-111, the fingerprint's path parameters are OB-112, and the reading of `UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE` was **wrong** (libuv reads it with `atoi`, so `0`, `abc` and an empty value are one thread, not four) and is corrected and measured ([S0.16](#s016--bounded-hash-admission)).

**The gates of the batch, and the three failures to report.** The commands of the Commands section of `CLAUDE.md` ran uncached (`TURBO_FORCE=true`, Node 24.21.0, Windows) twice; the figures are in [§1.2](#12-current-position). The first gate ran on the batch as built. The review above then changed code, so the second ran on the tree that is handed over. (1) **`pnpm test` failed once and then passed unchanged.** On the first gate eleven of the 23 Ops Portal test files ended with `[vitest-pool]: Failed to start forks worker` (the 12 that started passed, 143 tests), and turbo cancelled the API run; the same command repeated alone, with nothing changed, passed in full, and on the second gate it passed at the first run. This is the start-up failure `DEVELOPMENT.md` already describes for this workstation; **its cause is not established here** (nothing was diagnosed, no timeout was raised, and a pass is not a diagnosis), and the open finding about this machine (§1.2, "Open owner items") stands. (2) **`test:db` failed once on the second gate, deterministically, for a defect of the review's own fix.** One test (`lets a SYSTEM transaction record the person it authenticated`) named, as the person, the id that the shared test fixture also gives the `SYSTEM` context as its technical identifier; the fix for finding 5 correctly refuses that. The test now gives that context an identifier of its own, as production does (SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.16); `format:check`, `lint`, `typecheck` and `test:db` were rerun on the final tree and passed. `build`, `contract:check`, `api-client:check`, `db:check`, `pnpm test` and `test:e2e` were not rerun after that one edit of a database test, which none of them reads. (3) **No `execution-budget.db.test.ts` failure occurred** in the three database-project runs (15 tests passed in that file each time; the whole project passed in two of them), so there was no recurrence to capture; the intermittent timer failures of Stage 1 remain unexplained (OB-91; [§7.12](#712-stage-1-correction-batch-10-october-2026-disposition)), and a pass is not a diagnosis.

**Engineering parameters of the batch and their qualification** (each *development-qualified, not production-qualified*; S0.65 calibrates them): the limiter's window (60 s) is the contract's and not a parameter; the limiter's four constants and the hash admission's slots, memory budget and reserved thread are in the rows and the deployment record above; the audit writer's bounds (`AUDIT_LIMITS` in `audit-vocabulary.ts`: 32 keys, depth 4, 512-character texts, 4096 bytes of structured facts, 1000-character notes) are screening limits of this writer and not product values; the metrics registry's diagnostic interval (60 s) likewise. The idempotency window of a replayed secret is the grant's expiry, which the caller supplies; no figure is chosen here.

**What the batch left for the Product Owner to decide** (the rows above, in the order they bite): OB-102 and OB-100 (before the pipeline, S0.20), OB-96 (before S0.34 and S0.49), OB-93, OB-94 and OB-95 (the audit contract's three disagreements), OB-103 (the metric names), OB-110 (the shared capacity, before authentication is exposed) and OB-111 (`recorded_at`, with release gate G1). No unresolved product decision stopped independent work.

### 7.14 Carried from S0.17 to S0.19 (batch of 10 October 2026)

The owner's instruction of 10 October 2026 authorized S0.17 to S0.19 as one bounded batch on top of S0.11 to S0.16, left uncommitted and unpushed for the owner's review of Stage 2 as a whole. **Gate 2 is not passed and is not claimed:** the worker's real `SIGTERM` stop on Linux, WSL2 or CI has not run (OB-24), and the other Linux, WSL2 and CI checks of [§7.2](#72-release-gates--not-acceptance-of-this-slices-code) and OB-108 remain owed. The evidence is *fixture/platform verified* (a real PostgreSQL 18.6, the technical logins, the kit); no operation of the contract exists, so none of it is *real-operation* evidence. The rows below are what the batch decided on its own authority or found in the documents and did not settle; none is a default for a product value, and none changes an identifier, the OpenAPI document, a generated client or a contract.

| ID| Kind| Obligation or finding| Source| Built and proved by| Run| State|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OB-113| Finding| **Who reclaims an `idempotency_record` after its window is undecided, and S0.19 reclaims nothing.** settings.md says a record "may be reclaimed" after `idempotency_record_ttl_hours` (24, seeded) and the registry gives no identity `DELETE` on the table. S0.19 does the part the registry allows: it erases the sealed replay of a record whose window has closed (`sealed_expires_at`), all of it together, sets the tombstone (`replay_unavailable_at`) and keeps the saved answer byte for byte. The rows stay, tombstones included, so the table grows. **The owner decides** which identity may delete a record past its window (a `DELETE` grant and a policy for a named capability, which amends the registry), or that rows are kept for a stated period| data-scope-registry.md §5.2; settings.md §7.1; SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.16| The Product Owner, before the first operation that stores an idempotency record is exposed| D| Open|
| OB-114| Finding| **The retention of a finished job's metadata is `OQ-028` and unset, so nothing deletes a `credential_delivery_job`.** A job that has ended (`ACCEPTED`, `FAILED`, `EXPIRED`, `CANCELLED`) keeps its identifiers, state, counts, times and a non-secret outcome class; its payload (destination, link, token) is erased when it ends and a CHECK holds that. No identity holds `DELETE` (registry) and no setting names a period. **How S0.19's stop condition ("Retention periods are unset in settings.md") was read:** the part of the task that would need an unset period (deleting finished jobs, and tombstoned records) is not built, and is the decision reported here; the independent part, erasing credential material whose own end has passed, uses only values that are set (a job's own end, which is its redemption record's expiry carried on the row, and a sealed replay's own window, `sealed_expires_at`), invents no duration and deletes nothing. The category periods (`OQ-028`, "needing legal input") stay the owner's. **The owner decides** the period and the identity that deletes a finished job| settings.md (the retention rows); BACKGROUND_JOBS_AND_EVENTS.md; `OQ-028`| The Product Owner, with legal input| D| Open|
| OB-115| Finding| **The registry says the API cannot read what it queued; PostgreSQL needs the key columns to be readable.** An `UPDATE … WHERE` (the cancellation) needs `SELECT` on the columns it reads. The API's login therefore holds `SELECT` on five columns of `credential_delivery_job` (the redemption record's type and id, the principal's type and id, and `state`) and on no other: never the payload, the destination, the lease, the outcome or even the job's own identifier, which no statement of the API reads. The `authentication` read policy admits every row and the column grant limits it. The registry's wording is not changed here; the owner may amend it to say so| data-scope-registry.md §4.6, §5.1| The Product Owner| D| Accepted for review|
| OB-116| Engineering choice| **The ten outcome classes.** The documents leave the closed, non-secret `outcome_class` to the build and name one (`DESTINATION_NOT_ALLOWED`). Chosen: `ACCEPTED`, `UNKNOWN`, `REJECTED`, `DESTINATION_NOT_ALLOWED`, `SCHEDULE_EXHAUSTED`, `PAYLOAD_UNREADABLE`, `EXPIRED`, `CANCELLED`, `RELINQUISHED` and `REDEMPTION_UNCONFIRMED`. The table's CHECKs pair each with the states it may accompany, and the metric counts the four that end in `FAILED` (`credential_delivery_jobs_failed_total{outcome}`). A class never carries a destination, a link, a token, a provider's message or an identifier. The owner confirms or renames them| SECURITY_DESIGN.md §13.9a; state-machines.md §22; OBSERVABILITY_AND_RECOVERY.md §3| S0.17, S0.18| B| Accepted for review|
| OB-117| Engineering choice| **The worker's settings, its login and its least privilege.** New names, for the owner to confirm: `DATABASE_WORKER_URL` (the login `melarc_worker_runtime`; **forbidden in the API's environment**, where a handler could reach the capability that reads the sealed payload of every queued message), `DELIVERY_WORKER_CONCURRENCY` (4), `DELIVERY_WORKER_POLL_INTERVAL_MS` (2000), `DELIVERY_CAPTURE_DIRECTORY`, `DELIVERY_PROVIDER_APPROVAL_REF` and `DELIVERY_PROVIDER_ALLOWLIST`. A deployed worker holds the `credential_delivery` key and no other, and refuses `DATABASE_URL`, the key material of any other purpose and `ATTESTATION_VERIFIER_CALLER_SECRET`. **OB-52 is read as:** the runtime worker never needs the administrator's password; a local run derives its login the way `db:seed` does (`localLoginPassword`), for a server on the machine only, and a deployed environment gives it a secret of its own (G3). The table is DEPLOYMENT_AND_ENVIRONMENTS.md §12.5| OB-52; SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.9| S0.18| The Product Owner (the names); G3 (the secrets)| D| Accepted for review|
| OB-118| Engineering choice| **Unknown outcomes, the schedule and the port.** A timeout, a thrown error, a lost acknowledgement and any answer that is not one of the three is `UNKNOWN`: the job is retried with the same link and the same deduplication identity on the schedule of BACKGROUND_JOBS_AND_EVENTS.md §3.2, and before an attempt after the first (or the takeover of a lease) the worker asks the provider's status where it has one. Chosen where the documents are silent: the jitter is ±20 %; a stop that gives a lease back (`RELINQUISHED`) **counts as an attempt** and the job is due again at once; a record that cannot be read ends the attempt as `REDEMPTION_UNCONFIRMED`, which waits on the schedule and sends nothing; a provider's `Retry-After` may lengthen a wait up to 2 hours and never removes the bound. **The schedule is six attempts, and a lease taken over after a crash and a lease given back at a stop each count as one:** a message claimed a seventh time is asked about once more (the provider may hold it) and then ends as `SCHEDULE_EXHAUSTED`, and nothing is sent. A job given back at its sixth attempt can therefore end without a sixth real send; the owner may prefer that a lease given back before the provider was asked not count. A payload that only this worker cannot open (a key, envelope or layout version it has not been given, as in a rotation under way) is kept and tried again on the schedule rather than erased. The port of S0.1 changed to carry this: the unknown answer of `DeliveryOutcome` may carry `retryAfterMs`, `RecordedOutcome` is new (with `SCHEDULE_EXHAUSTED`), `recordOutcome` returns the `JobEnd`, and the error of an unreadable claim carries why; the descriptor was updated and the describe composition is still inert| BACKGROUND_JOBS_AND_EVENTS.md §3.2; SECURITY_DESIGN.md §13.9a| S0.17, S0.18| The Product Owner| B| Accepted for review|
| OB-119| Binding| **The worker sends nothing until the redemption records exist.** The reader of a record's standing (`RedemptionRecordReader`: state and expiry only, never the hash) has no implementation. `UNBUILT_REDEMPTION_READER` confirms nothing, so every attempt ends `REDEMPTION_UNCONFIRMED`, nothing reaches the capture sink, and the worker says so when it starts. The real readers arrive with S0.29 (`SetupGrant`) and S0.50 (`RecoveryRequest`), each with the grant `credential_delivery` holds on its table (registry: "state and expiry only"). Jobs queued before then wait on the schedule and end at their record's expiry| data-scope-registry.md §5.2; SECURITY_DESIGN.md §13.9a| S0.29, S0.50| D| Not built|
| OB-120| Release| **No real provider is built, and the provider timeouts are not qualified.** The providers are external deployment inputs no document has selected, and the first is a prerequisite of release gate G3. The allow-list guard of the controlled window (`AllowListedDeliveryProvider`: `DESTINATION_NOT_ALLOWED` before any provider call) is built and tested against a stand-in provider| DEPLOYMENT_AND_ENVIRONMENTS.md §4.1| G3| D| Not built|

The worker ends such a job as `EXPIRED` once the record's end has passed and as `CANCELLED` before it, on the database's clock, in the statement that records it. So that this does not become a power to cancel the queue, the guard lets the worker's capability end only a job it holds the lease of, and the consuming operation's capability cancels a queued one. Nothing can be sent today, because no provider adapter is built (OB-120). **The owner decides**, before G3 builds the first adapter, whether a local run refuses a real provider altogether, accepts one only on a loopback endpoint (a local stand-in server), or may use one inside the controlled window| SECURITY_DESIGN.md §13.9a; DEPLOYMENT_AND_ENVIRONMENTS.md §4, §12.3| The Product Owner, before G3| D| Closed by the owner's decision B of 11 October 2026 (Local and CI use capture only, no local exception): refused on the environment (`deliveryAdapterProblems`, shared by the API and the worker) and again on the built configuration (`adapterRefusals`, the worker's refusals), with tests; staging's controlled window and production's real-provider rules are unchanged (§7.15)|
| OB-128| Binding| **A session is issued on the credential the caller verified, named by its generation (`credential_generation`), and refused (`CREDENTIAL_CHANGED`) when the identity holds another under its lock.** Not by `credential_established_at`: two credentials can carry the same instant (a clock repeats and steps back, and a date keeps milliseconds where the database keeps microseconds). A trigger holds the generation: it rises by exactly one and never moves back, and `credential_established_at` changes only with a new generation and may equal the previous one (a replacement at the very instant of the one it replaced is a replacement all the same). That holds only if **every replacement of a credential raises the generation by one and sets the moment, and a rehash after a successful verification changes neither** (setup S0.31, recovery S0.50 and S0.51, a reset, a change); a replacer that did not raise the generation, or that changed the moment alone, is refused by the trigger. The database cannot tell a replacement from a rehash by the hash string, so the writers keep the two apart. The sign-ins (S0.32, S0.48, S0.59) pass the generation they read in the transaction that verified. | OB-131| Binding| **A wait on a lock cannot revive an expired idle session, and cannot authorize a state-changing operation on stale authority.** `SessionAuthenticator` reads the session, takes the database time, reads the idle settings, judges, and then records the activity; the update can wait on a competing transaction, and its result is not a revalidation of the earlier read. A request may linearize before a revocation that overlaps it, and that is not a defect; but the complete pipeline must prove it: hold the lock a revocation or an expiry takes, let a request wait on it, release it, and show the request is judged on a fresh time and the locked state. S0.24 accepts such a session: authentication judges expiry and idleness only and takes no policy lock. If the check lands in the authorization slot, as S0.26's text reads, an unsafe request on such a session meets the CSRF `403` first and has already recorded its activity. **Recommendation:** make the floor comparison a part of the authentication step (under the shared policy lock for a state-changing request), so that the refusal order stays one list. The owner confirms before S0.26| SECURITY_DESIGN.md §13.10, §13.10a; permission-enforcement.md §5.1; plan S0.26, S0.27| The Product Owner, before S0.26| D| Closed (12 October 2026, decision C): the floor comparison is part of the authentication step, a below-floor session is `401 SESSION_INVALID` before CSRF, an instance that cannot evaluate current policy answers the `503` and ends nothing, and a state-changing request repeats the check under the shared locks (`authority.db.test.ts`, `policy-change-races.db.test.ts`; [§7.18](#718-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026))|

**Review of the batch (10 October 2026) and what it changed.** Two read-only reviewers read the batch against the registry, the contract and the design, one the database side and one the worker, the process, the configuration and the documents (they could run nothing). Nothing they reported was taken on trust: each finding was checked against the code and the documents and, where it held, fixed test first, the new test being seen to fail for the stated reason before the change, or replaced if it could not fail. What held:
- *Medium, both reviewers:* the six-attempt bound was applied only when an unknown outcome was recorded, so a lease taken over after a crash at the sixth attempt, or given back at the sixth at a stop, led to a seventh send, and again for each lease that ended until the credential expired. A message claimed after the last attempt is now asked about and ends, and nothing is sent (OB-118).
- *Medium:* a payload that only this worker could not open (a key, envelope or layout version it had not been given: a rotation under way) was erased and its job failed for good. It is kept and tried again on the schedule; only a payload no worker can open ends the job.
- *High, worker reviewer:* a call was declared late by a timer of its own, which can fire a millisecond before the budget's monotonic clock agrees, so that the next call could start on time that was spent (a test of that case could fail where timers are precise, as on Linux, which was not run), and the signal of the abandoned call's budget was never aborted. A call is now late when its budget says so (shown with a clock that runs at half speed), and the signal is aborted.
- *Medium:* after a shutdown had given a lease back, a lane it had given up on could go on to send (a status query that answered late, then a send) from a process that had reported that it stopped. The budget every job hangs from is finished at the stop and the calls the lanes wait on are let go.
- *Low, real:* `REDEMPTION_CLOSED` read the database clock twice (the state and the class could disagree on either side of the moment the credential ended, and the table refuses the pair); the job's time was counted from the end of the claim though the lease begins inside it; the record was read before the provider's status query and not just before the send; the cleanup ran under no budget of its own and did not leave off at a stop, and a lease that ended on a job the cleanup expired was never counted; `enqueue` and `cancel` under another context than the issuing capability's returned as if nothing was live; the guard let an outcome replace a payload, let the worker's capability cancel jobs it never leased and take a lease of years, and the erase policy of sealed replays did not keep to the window; the API held `SELECT` on the job's identifier, which none of its statements reads (OB-115); the capture sink's folder was judged on its written path (a link into the checkout passed) and its mode was not checked; a configuration made by hand skipped the refusals the loader makes; the shutdown report counted every lease unconfirmed if one could not be given back, could go negative, and an unreadable claim was recorded after the stop; a refusal of `NODE_ENV` told the operator to choose among three modes; and four statements were stale (the number of tests Windows skips, "the only code that opens the capability", a refusal missing from `.env.example`, and "exercised by" cells that no test supported).
- *Test quality, real:* a test that could not fail (a fence phrased with the wrong version), a claimed catch that no test made (an outcome that changes who holds the lease), a test named for a case it did not attempt, "never touches the redemption record" assertions that no code could break (replaced by the shape of the table), a fake queue that never read the budget it ran under, claim and outcome budgets and the back-off cap exercised by no test, a lease-arithmetic test that compared the function with itself, and an assertion that accepted either of two outcomes for a case that has one.
- *Reported and not built:* the worker, not the consuming operation, ends a job whose record closed (OB-126); Local and CI use only the capture adapter by §13.9a, and the loader allows a real provider in a local run (OB-127).

**Targeted breaks of the review's changes.** 61 single breaks (the late call, the stop, the attempt limit, the claim's anchor, the version skew, the periodic task's budget and stop, the shutdown report, the statements of the store, the guard's new rules, the grant, the queue's check of the context, the erase window, the count of leases, the sink's links and permission bits, the configuration's relations, and the runtime's wiring): 55 were caught at first; four gaps (the release of a lane the stop gave up on, a lease that was no longer the worker's, a claim that comes back after the stop, and the group-write bit of the sink's folder) were closed with tests and caught on a second run; two are equivalent mutants (the check of the principal's type, which the check of the capability implies, and the lexical comparison of the sink's path, which the resolved one subsumes wherever the operating system can resolve it).

**One observation of the environment, not of a reviewer.** The database container's clock was ahead of the host's by about 76 ms (75.3 to 76.2 over 40 round trips, measured on 10 October 2026), and one test of this batch that compared the host's `Date.now()` with a time the database wrote, with 50 ms of slack, failed on it; it now compares on the database's clock. Any test that compares the two clocks is sensitive to that offset on this machine. The cause of the intermittent `execution-budget.db.test.ts` failures (OB-91) is not established here, and this is not offered as it.

**The gate of the batch, and the one failure to report.** The commands of the Commands section of `CLAUDE.md` ran uncached (`TURBO_FORCE=true`, Node 24.21.0, Windows) once, on the tree handed over, after the review's changes; the figures are in [§1.2](#12-current-position). (1) **`pnpm test` failed once and then passed unchanged.** In the first run eleven of the 23 Ops Portal test files ended with `[vitest-pool]: Failed to start forks worker ... Timeout waiting for worker to respond` (the 12 that started passed, 143 tests), and turbo cancelled the API run; the same command repeated alone, uncached and with nothing changed, passed in full. This is the start-up failure `DEVELOPMENT.md` already describes for this workstation and the first gate of the previous batch showed; the batch touches nothing of the Ops Portal, **its cause is not established here** (nothing was diagnosed beyond that, no timeout was raised, and a pass is not a diagnosis), and the open finding about this machine (§1.2, "Open owner items") stands. (2) **No `execution-budget.db.test.ts` failure occurred** (15 tests passed in the full `test:db` run), so there was nothing to capture; the intermittent timer failures of Stage 1 remain unexplained (OB-91; [§7.12](#712-stage-1-correction-batch-10-october-2026-disposition)), and a pass is not a diagnosis. (3) **Skipped, not passed:** eight API tests of `pnpm test` (the five real-`SIGTERM` process tests of Stage 1, the delivery worker's real-`SIGTERM` process test, and the two tests of the capture sink's file and folder modes, which a Windows file system cannot answer) and two of `test:db` (`api-process.db.test.ts` and `delivery-worker-process.db.test.ts`, real `SIGTERM`); they run on Linux, WSL2 or CI, and the worker's two are what Gate 2 waits for (OB-24).

**Engineering parameters of the batch and their qualification** (each *development-qualified, not production-qualified*; S0.65 calibrates them): the worker's lanes (4), poll interval (2 s), lease base (30 s) and margin (5 s), claim and outcome budgets (10 s and 5 s), capture-call bound (5 s), relinquish reserve (2 s) and back-off cap (30 s), the schedule's jitter (±20 %; the schedule itself is the document's), the cleanup's batch (200), interval (60 s) and rounds (20), the allow-list's bound (50 entries), and the limits on a destination and a link (`delivery-payload.ts`). They are capacity and screening limits chosen here and not product values. The measured figures, with the machine and the tests that exercise each, are in DEPLOYMENT_AND_ENVIRONMENTS.md §12.5.

**What the batch left for the Product Owner to decide** (the rows above, in the order they bite): OB-113 and OB-114 (which identity may delete, and the periods: before the first operation that stores an idempotency record is exposed, and before any finished-job metadata is to be deleted), OB-115 and OB-116 (the registry's wording; the outcome-class names), OB-117 (the worker's setting names), OB-118, OB-124, OB-125 and OB-126 (the points the schedule's document is silent on; the capture sink's place and form; whether the cleanup has a capability of its own; who ends a job whose record closed) and OB-127 (whether a local run may use a real provider, before G3). OB-102 and OB-100 of [§7.13](#713-carried-from-s011-to-s016-batch-of-10-october-2026) remain the ones that bite first (before S0.20). No unresolved product decision stopped independent work.

### 7.15 Gate 2 audit corrections (11 October 2026): disposition

The audit of S0.11 to S0.19 (the supplied review; no separate audit file exists in the repository) found eight defects and five small gaps; a final bounded follow-up (below) closed what they left open. This section is the one record of what was reproduced, what was changed, what proves it and what remains. It is a correction batch on the tree of the two batches above: nothing is staged, committed or pushed, S0.20 is not started, no dependency changed, `melarc_dev` and every local secret were left alone. **Gate 2 is not passed and is not claimed**: the worker's real `SIGTERM` stop and the capture sink's mode tests are Linux evidence that has not run on this tree (OB-24, no waiver).

**Method.** Each defect was reproduced against the real code before it was changed (a failing test, or a plan taken from the real database), and fixed at its cause. Deliberate breaks of each new guard were run and restored by hash. Three of the breaks were *equivalent mutants* and are named as such below, not counted as caught.

**The owner's decisions of the brief, and what each did.** A (architecture kept): nothing was redesigned; the worker, the queue, lease fencing and purpose-separated keys are as they were. B (Local and CI capture only): §11.A below; closes OB-127. C (the limiter stays database-authoritative): G2-04, G2-05, §11.B; no per-process limiter exists. D (missing key or version support is operational): G2-03. E (tiers fixed centrally; conditional ones by explicit validated context; a read fabricates no before and after): G2-06. F (event identifiers kept; grammar and counts corrected): G2-06, `audit.md` §3 to §6. G (erasure under `credential_delivery` with narrow column privileges): G2-07, §11.E; no new grant of any kind except the one column `deferred_claims` (G2-03) and the one `SELECT` policy of §11.B. H (nothing chosen for retention periods, deletion, the idempotency HTTP error or other unspecified product values): §11.C to §11.E. I (no authenticated work moved into `SYSTEM` to get past audit permissions): P-1 below.

| Finding| Reproduced| Corrected| Proof| Remaining limitation|
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **G2-01** capture-directory containment| A sink named `..capture` beside the checkout was judged inside it, because containment was a string prefix test on the relative path (`startsWith('..')`)| `isInside` judges whole path components (`outside-checkout.ts`); a folder on another Windows drive is outside| `outside-checkout.test.ts` (a child named with leading dots is inside, the parent and a sibling are not; another drive; the `..capture` sink is refused); the process test that a sink inside the checkout is refused and nothing is created; 1 break caught| The drive test runs on Windows only and is skipped elsewhere. Symlink and junction handling is the existing realpath resolution, unchanged|
| **G2-02** provider deadline settlement| A provider that answered after a call's own deadline, before any timer had fired, was taken as timely: six deterministic cases with a manual clock accepted a late answer| The worker settles a call as timely only when it was not spent **and** its budget, read on the clock, was not expired (`#call`); a late answer is recorded `UNKNOWN`, once| `delivery-worker.test.ts`, "a provider that answers after the call's own deadline, before any timer has noticed" (and the stop case: the let-go send counts once as `UNKNOWN`); 1 break caught| A late *acceptance* is recorded `UNKNOWN`, the conservative reading; the next attempt asks the provider whether it holds the message (reconciliation) before it sends|
| **G2-03** a payload this worker cannot open| Against the real queue, six claims by a worker without the payload's key version ended the job `FAILED` (`SCHEDULE_EXHAUSTED`), erased the payload and never asked a provider: a live credential message destroyed by an operational mismatch| A **deferral**: the outcome `PAYLOAD_DEFERRED` records that nothing was sent, gives the claim back (`deferred_claims`, migration `0011`; the sends so far are `attempt_count - deferred_claims`), keeps the payload and waits 30 s doubling to 10 min with jitter; only the credential's end (judged on the database's clock in the statement) or a cancellation ends the job. `REDEMPTION_UNCONFIRMED` takes the same path. A claim whose worker records nothing (a crash) still counts as an attempt. The guard trigger lets the count rise by one only on that transition and refuses a deferral that does not give its claim back. The worker slows its own claiming for each deferral in a row, counts `credential_delivery_jobs_deferred_total{reason}`, and **claims nothing at all while no redemption reader is built** (`consume: false`), so it spends nothing of any queued message. Unsupported version versus genuine failure to authenticate is the existing split (`NOT_HELD` versus `UNREADABLE`)| `delivery-worker.db.test.ts` (nine deferrals then a compatible worker sends the same message with the same identity; the wait doubles to the cap; the credential's end during a claim; cancellation of a deferred job; a crash is still an attempt; the sixth and seventh send); `credential-delivery-job.db.test.ts` (the guard, the bound, the grant); worker, runtime, schedule and metric unit tests; the process test that an unbuilt reader leaves a live message `QUEUED` with no attempt counted; 19 breaks of 20 caught on the first run and the twentieth is an equivalent mutant (the state conditions of the new guard rule repeat the transition rule that refuses first)| The wait parameters are development-qualified (S0.65). The deferral count has no cap by design; a deferral is bounded in time by the credential's end or a cancellation (stated precisely below). A new job is inserted untouched at the database boundary since migration `0014` (F-4, P-6 resolved)|
| **G2-04** limiter key rotation across instances| An instance holding key version 1 admitted a key's whole allowance again after an instance holding versions 1 and 2 had used it under version 2: they shared a lock, which orders two decisions and proves nothing about what each can count| A **staged protocol** (SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.12, DEPLOYMENT_AND_ENVIRONMENTS.md): distribute, establish, switch, retain, retire. At run time the limiter **refuses to decide, failing closed**, while any live row is under a version it does not hold (`LimiterKeyVersionError` inside `LIMITER_STORE_UNAVAILABLE`, counted in `rate_limiter_key_incompatible_total`); one range probe per stretch of unheld versions on a new index (`rate_limit_admission_version_idx`, migration `0012`)| `rate-limiter.db.test.ts`: instances holding the same versions and writing under different ones share one allowance, alternating and concurrent; the first stage (distributed, not active) changes nothing; the asymmetric old/new state is refused and writes nothing; a version retired while rows under it are live is refused and admits once they leave the window; a version between two held ones; the refusal is counted; the plan of the probe at 20000 rows reads the version index and no table scan; 8 breaks caught. **The follow-up (F-1)** closed the race between the check and the insert: the check is repeated under the capacity lock| **What the run-time guard proves:** an instance inserts only while it can count every live row, the version check being repeated under the capacity lock immediately before the insert. **What it does not prove, and the deployment owes:** step 2 of the protocol (every serving instance holds the new version before any writes under it), the retirement discipline of step 4, anything about an instance it cannot see (one not yet started, or running a release that predates the check) and the availability of an excluded instance, which refuses every admission until it holds the version. A release that rotates the key records the check; no platform check exists|
| **G2-05** limiter storage capacity| Capacity was judged on rows already committed: with a capacity of 5, 30 distinct keys arriving through two pools admitted 8; with 12, 16. An expired backlog was not counted at all| One **capacity lock** (`RATE_LIMIT_CAPACITY`, taken last, after the key locks): under it the admission removes a bounded batch of the oldest expired rows, counts **every** row (live or expired) up to the capacity and inserts. No stored counter exists, so a rollback, a lost commit or a crash leaves nothing to correct; only a transaction holding the lock deletes, so no delete waits for another's rows; the cleanup election is removed (see OB-109). The limits are read in the same transaction (§11.B)| `rate-limiter.db.test.ts`: exactly the capacity is admitted for capacities 5 and 12 through two independent pools; a lost commit gives the place back; expired rows still there count, and a bounded number is reclaimed; an expired backlog above the capacity refuses uniformly until reclaimed below it; a victim's live rows survive a concurrent flood; a refusal at a ceiling does not wait for the lock; the batch is bounded and oldest first; 6 breaks caught; the first run missed one (capacity judged on live rows only), which showed a missing test, and it was caught once the test was added (a seventh edit was a no-op and is discarded)| **Brief global coordination, stated plainly:** admissions that write queue for the capacity lock for the length of a cleanup, a count, an insert and the commit. Measured on the S0.18 machine: eight callers with their own keys, **100 admissions a second against 233 before**; one admission 18.3 ms (empty table) and 34.8 ms (19000 live rows); refusals at a ceiling do not take the lock. This is the price of the strict bound and is not a deployed-topology figure: **P-3**. An instance still running the previous release does not take the lock, so the bound is strict only once every instance runs this one. How capacity is shared between buckets is not decided here|
| **G2-06** audit tier and facts| `auth.mfa.reset` was accepted with `enhanced: false`, no reason, no reset lineage and no before and after| `audit-classification.ts`: every one of the 190 codes has a tier read from its catalogue row (76 enhanced, 109 ordinary, 5 that depend on the act), enforced by the writer: an enhanced event cannot be written ordinary, an ordinary one cannot be written enhanced, and the five (`auth.recovery.requested`, `staff.identity.created`, `delivery.stop.failed`, `vendor.sender_block.created` and, since the follow-up, `auth.session.terminated`, enhanced when an administrator ended another principal's session) must state a basis that fixes the tier and, where the transaction can say (an administrator-initiated recovery is a staff transaction; the seed is the `seed` capability), is checked against it. Whether an event carries a before and after is decided by the change it records and not by its tier (F-3, below). For the identity events whose requirements the catalogue states clearly (`auth.mfa.reset`, `auth.session.authority_revoked`, `auth.session.terminated`, `auth.recovery.requested`, `auth.hub_boundary.crossed`, `staff.identity.approved`, `staff.identity.rejected`) the reason, the states, the lineage keys the catalogue names and the actor type are required; no key name the catalogue does not give was invented. A null object is allowed for the two events that describe a case without one. `audit.md` is corrected: 190 codes (it said 188), the domains (`auth` and `order` are outside the permission list, recorded and not decided), ten identifiers that end in a state and not a verb, five enhanced authentication events (it said four), the before and after rule| `audit-classification.test.ts` (the document agrees with the table row by row; the counts; each fact refused for its own field; the bases and the cross-checks; the null object; the reason and lineage keys), with `audit-diff-policy.test.ts` and `audit-mfa-reset.test.ts` of the follow-up, and `audit-event.db.test.ts` (a caller that catches a refusal of the new rules still cannot commit the act); 18 breaks caught on the first pass| The two bundles of `permission.bundle.assigned` are still required by presence only. The bases `PAYMENT_TAKEN` and `PLATFORM_WIDE` cannot be checked here and are the emitting operation's to state truthfully. The table's own `CHECK` does not restrict a null object id (the writer is the one door). The facts of an MFA reset are an explicit shape and prove no lineage (F-2). **Vendor and Rider access: P-1**|
| **G2-07** replay expiry index| The plan of the cleanup's own statement, as `melarc_worker_runtime` under `credential_delivery` with the real policies, on 80000 records (60000 sealed, 50 due, 20000 erased): a sequential scan and a sort, 79950 rows removed by the filter, 2703 pages for the scan and 3639 for the statement, to find 50| Migration `0013`: a partial index on `(sealed_expires_at, id) WHERE sealed_expires_at IS NOT NULL` (a fixed condition, no clock); the cleanup bounds its window with `statement_timestamp()`, which is stable and so can descend the index (`clock_timestamp()` is volatile and made it a filter), while the row policy of `0010` still judges each row on `clock_timestamp()`, so an erasure is never early| `retention-cleanup.db.test.ts` (the plan, with the planner left to choose, no setting forced): the index scan, no sequential scan, **934 pages** for the statement including erasing the 50, and **5 pages** when nothing is due; the index predicate is pinned; the bounded batch, `SKIP LOCKED`, the tombstone and the saved answer are unchanged (the existing 18 tests); 3 breaks caught| Indicative, on one machine, at this size|
| **G2-08** metrics refusal| `increment` with a `null` labels argument threw a `TypeError` from `Object.keys(null)` with production's drop behaviour selected, and an empty array was accepted as a record of no labels| The container is judged before it is enumerated (`isRecord`): a record is a non-null, non-array object (a null-prototype object and a class instance are records); anything else is a label-set mismatch, dropped and counted in production, `LABEL_UNBOUNDED` in development and test; no value is copied into a diagnostic; a sink that fails stays contained| `bounded.metrics.adapter.test.ts`: null, arrays, text, numbers, booleans, functions, symbols, bigint, for counters and observations, in both policies; `undefined` still means no labels; 3 breaks caught| The boundary is a record of plain data. A `Proxy` or a getter that throws is the caller running hostile code in the process and is not guarded against|

**The five small gaps.**

- **A. Local and CI capture only (closes OB-127).** `deliveryAdapterProblems` (shared by the API and the worker loaders) refuses `DELIVERY_*_ADAPTER=provider` when `APP_ENV` is `local`; `adapterRefusals` (the API's built configuration) and the worker's refusals refuse it again on a configuration made by hand. Staging's controlled window and production's real-provider requirements are untouched. Tests: `load-config.test.ts`, `worker-config.test.ts`, `delivery-worker-runtime.test.ts`, `app.module.test.ts`; `DEPLOYMENT_AND_ENVIRONMENTS.md` §12.3 and `.env.example` say so.
- **B. The limiter's six settings.** Migration `0012` adds the policy `setting_rate_limit_read`: the `rate_limit` capability may `SELECT` the six `rate_limit_*` rows and no other row of `setting`, and writes nothing (the runtime login holds no write on `setting`). The limiter reads its limit in its own admission transaction, so an admission takes one connection, not two (`SettingsBucketLimits.limitOf(bucket, transaction)`). `settings-reason.db.test.ts` proves the six are readable, every other setting reads as missing, the visible rows are exactly the six, and insert, update and delete are refused; the policy's names are pinned to `RATE_LIMIT_BUCKETS`. The registry's `rate_limit` row (`data-scope-registry.md` §5.2) is amended. **The brief said "prepare or apply"; this is applied, under it, and is the owner's to confirm or reverse (OB-102).** One equivalent mutant: the policy's capability condition is defence in depth, because the only API-login capabilities are `authentication` (which already reads every setting) and `rate_limit`.
- **C. `recorded_at`.** Closed by the owner's decision on P-2 (F-5, migration `0015`): the value is the moment of the insert. Neither `recorded_at` nor the event `id` is a commit-order cursor for the signed checkpoint chain (membership is explicit, `audit.md` §2.1).
- **D. A command in progress under its key.** SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.16 now records how `begin`, the business effect and `complete` in one transaction leave no committed `IN_PROGRESS` record; that a legitimate concurrent caller does not observe `IN_PROGRESS` (its `begin` waits on the unique index and then replays or owns the command); that `IN_PROGRESS` can be reported only for a record committed without its `complete`, a defect of whoever assembled the command; that the pipeline of S0.20 must make the three steps one unit of work; and that **the contract code and HTTP answer for `IN_PROGRESS`, and who reclaims a stranded record, remain the owner's decisions (OB-100, section 7.14)**. No error code was invented.
- **E. Retention.** DEPLOYMENT_AND_ENVIRONMENTS.md separates three things: erasing credential material (done), the replay guarantees (a saved answer byte for byte; a sealed replay disclosed only until its window closes) and the retention of metadata (a finished job's state, and the reclaiming of an idempotency record; both periods unset, no deletion granted). **S0.19 is complete for the first and for the sealed-replay half of the second; it is not complete as a whole** while OB-113, OB-114 and `OQ-028` are open.

**The "Timeout waiting for worker to respond" investigation.** Its record, with the checks of the final tree, is at the end of this section.

**The final bounded follow-up (11 October 2026).** Its scope, as the owner bounded it: the one concurrency gap the limiter still had, the facts of `auth.mfa.reset`, enhanced events that record no change, the initial state of a queued job, and the disposition of P-1 to P-7. The findings came in the owner's brief; no separate review file exists in the repository and none was read. As before, nothing is staged, committed or pushed, S0.20 is not started, no dependency changed, and `melarc_dev`, the running services and every local secret were left alone. The limiter race, the queue's insert and `recorded_at` were each reproduced as a failing test before they were changed. The audit shape and the diff rules were written with their tests in one pass, so their evidence is the tests and the deliberate breaks recorded under *The checks of the follow-up*.

| Finding| Evidence| Corrected| Proof| Remaining limitation|
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F-1** the limiter's compatibility check and its insert were not one step| Two instances whose key versions do not overlap (A holds only version 1, B only version 2) share no key lock. At a limit of 1 with ample capacity both passed the version check on an empty table and, once each had finished its check, both were admitted. The test is deterministic: an external session holds the capacity lock, the two contenders are started after it is held, and neither can insert before both have checked| The version check is repeated **under the capacity lock, immediately before the insert** (`postgres.rate-limiter.adapter.ts`), where the rows committed by the previous holder of the lock are visible. The lock order is unchanged (key locks ascending, the capacity lock last), so no cycle is added| `rate-limiter.db.test.ts` (44 tests): the disjoint-version race admits exactly one and the other fails closed with `LimiterKeyVersionError` and writes nothing; compatible instances that hold both versions but write under different ones still share one allowance and admit exactly one at a limit of 1; ordinary contention for the capacity (capacity 3, six contenders) admits exactly three, and its waiters hold only key locks (read from `pg_locks`)| What the guard proves and what the deployment owes are stated under G2-04 above and in SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.12|
| **F-2** the facts of `auth.mfa.reset` were any non-empty objects| The writer asked of the event a reason, the states and *some* `related_ids` and *some* `before_after`; `{ episode, new_grant }` and `{ factor: 'ACTIVE_TO_REVOKED' }` on an object of type `MfaFactor` passed| One explicit, validated shape (`audit-mfa-reset.ts`), documented as *Facts of `auth.mfa.reset`* in `audit.md` §5.4: the target is a `StaffIdentity` that is not the actor; the three states of state-machines.md §18 with their reason; `COALESCED` only for `UNFINISHED_RESET`; `related_ids` holds exactly `episode_id`, `revoked_factor_id` (present only where a factor was revoked), `new_grant_id` (present when issued) and `superseded_grant_ids` (always a list of distinct identifiers, empty when nothing was superseded, never naming the new grant); the diff is exactly what the branch changed. An unrelated object, an unknown key, a wrong type, an impossible combination or a secret-named field is refused for its own field, and a refusal still aborts the transaction| `audit-mfa-reset.test.ts` (32 tests): each branch's valid example, ISSUED against COALESCED, factor absent only where none exists, the empty list, the diff values, the reasons, the target and the actor, secret-named fields; `audit-event.db.test.ts`: a complete reset is stored with its lineage and diff, and two malformed ones are refused with the act rolled back| **The writer validates the shape and proves no lineage:** a well-formed identifier says nothing about whether the episode, the factor or the grant exists or belongs to the target. By branch: `auth.mfa.reset` and `auth.recovery.requested` carry none when `COALESCED` (nothing was issued, revoked or created) and carry the diff when `ISSUED` (for recovery, required when an administrator initiated it, optional when the principal did). `auth.session.terminated` becomes a conditional event, enhanced when an administrator ended another principal's session (`AC-SLICE-000-99`, §6 category 6): five conditional events, 76 enhanced and 109 ordinary of 190| `audit-diff-policy.test.ts` (21 tests): the six no-diff events are accepted with their own facts and refused with a diff; every change-recording enhanced code is refused without its diff, and accepts one; an attempt that changed nothing forbids a diff; an ordinary event is not held to one; the conditional branches; the administrator's revocation and the sign-out; the one rule function; and `audit.md`'s no-diff list equals `NO_DIFF_CODES`. `audit-classification.test.ts` and `audit-event.db.test.ts` follow the changes| **For the owner, `AC-SLICE-000-93`:** it requires an enhanced audit record "under the reserved system actor" when the provisioning-only resume issues a fresh `MFA_REENROLMENT`, and the event catalogue names no event for it (`auth.mfa.reset` is a Platform Admin's act on another identity). The canonical serialisation is unchanged (the same field, written as the same microsecond UTC text), so `serialisation_version` stays 1; `audit.md` §3 says so| `audit-event.db.test.ts`: both events of one transaction are later than `transaction_timestamp()` and the second is later than the first. No sleep: a statement round trip separates the inserts and the clock reads microseconds| Neither `recorded_at` nor the event `id` is a commit-order cursor. **The delivery deferral, stated precisely (G2-03).** A claim ends as a deferral only when the worker can confirm that it was unable to ask a provider (it could not open the sealed payload for want of a key, envelope or layout version it was not given, or it could not confirm the redemption record) and says so. Such a claim does **not** spend one of the six sends the Class 1 schedule allows: the sends so far are `attempt_count - deferred_claims`. A claim whose outcome is uncertain (the provider was asked and the answer is unknown, an answer after the call's own deadline, a lease that ended with no outcome, a crash) is counted as a send, conservatively, because it may have sent. The wait after a deferral is bounded (30 seconds doubling to a 10-minute cap, with jitter; development-qualified, S0.65), and the worker slows its own claiming while deferrals follow one another. **A deferred message does not wait for ever:** the job ends when its credential does (judged on the database's clock in the statement that claims it: `EXPIRED`, payload erased) or when it is cancelled (consumed, superseded or invalidated: `CANCELLED`, payload erased). The count of deferrals has no cap of its own, because a cap would be a product value nobody has set. While no redemption reader is built (`consume: false`; the reader arrives with S0.29 and S0.50) the worker claims nothing, so it spends nothing of any queued message. A deferral that persists is visible without a secret: `credential_delivery_jobs_deferred_total{reason}` and the job's non-secret `outcome_class` and `deferred_claims`, never the destination or the token.

**Disposition of P-1 to P-7 (the owner's decisions of 11 October 2026).**

- **P-1. Audit `INSERT` for Vendor and Rider: the narrow direction is approved; it is built by the first consumer, not now.** *Scope as approved.* Today only `STAFF` and `SYSTEM` under `authentication` may insert into `audit_event` (migration `0006`). An authenticated Vendor or Rider operation that must audit in its own transaction (`signOut`; a vendor's `requestAdditionalDeviceGrant` and `revokeVendorDevice`) cannot, and running it as `SYSTEM` is refused (decision I). The approved arms preserve the authenticated actor and the same transaction: in `audit_event_insert`, `WHEN 'VENDOR' THEN actor_type = 'VENDOR_ACCOUNT' AND actor_id = melarc.current_principal_id() AND surface_context = 'VENDOR_PWA' AND event_code IN ('auth.session.terminated', 'auth.setup_grant.issued', 'auth.device.revoked')` and `WHEN 'RIDER' THEN actor_type = 'RIDER' AND actor_id = melarc.current_principal_id() AND surface_context = 'RIDER_ANDROID' AND event_code = 'auth.session.terminated'`; no `SELECT` is added; the registry's `AuditEvent` row (§4.5) is amended to say the two arms. *First consumer:* S0.34 (`signOut`), and S0.49 for the vendor device operations. *Tests owed before that consumer is accepted* (database tests as `melarc_api_runtime`): a vendor or rider inserts only its own listed events, and nothing under another actor identifier, actor type, surface or event code; neither can read, change or remove any row of the ledger; the staff and `authentication` arms are unchanged; the refusal of an event aborts the act's transaction. *Bootstrap identities:* the audit of their creation (`staff.identity.created`, basis `BOOTSTRAP_SEED`) belongs **in the transaction that creates them**, not at their first sign-in. The authority for that transaction to write the event (a `seed` or provisioning insert policy or function, which does not exist and which the provisioning login does not hold) is recorded as a **prerequisite of the bootstrap task (S0.30)**; it is not designed or granted here. Closes the decision OB-96 asks for.
- **P-2. `recorded_at`: applied** as F-5 (insertion time, migration `0015`, existing rows preserved, serialisation version 1). Closes OB-111.
- **P-3. The limiter's throughput: the strict design is kept, provisionally.** Measured on the S0.18 machine (Windows 11, 6 cores and 12 threads, 15.9 GB, PostgreSQL 18.6 in a local container): eight callers with their own keys admitted **100 a second against 233 before the capacity lock**; one admission took 18.3 ms with an empty table and 34.8 ms with 19000 live rows; a refusal at a ceiling takes no lock. That is one machine and one topology, development-qualified and not a deployed figure, and no optimisation was made. If it is not enough at the deployed topology (S0.65, release gate G2), the options are a shared advisory lock while the table is far from its capacity and an exclusive one near it (a design with its own proof, sketched and not built), or a reserved share of the capacity per bucket (a product and capacity decision). Neither is chosen.
- **P-4. The answer for a command in progress (OB-100): still pending, to be decided before S0.20.** No code was written and no error code invented; `STATE_CONFLICT` is the nearest existing code and is not chosen. SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.16 records how the pipeline must make `begin`, the business effect and `complete` one unit of work.
- **P-5. Enhanced events that change nothing: resolved** by F-3.
- **P-6. The queue's insert: resolved** by F-4.
- **P-7. The registry amendment of §11.B: accepted by the owner.** Migration `0012` lets the `rate_limit` capability read the six `rate_limit_*` settings and no other row; the registry's `rate_limit` row says so. Closes OB-102.

**What is in which state after the follow-up.**

- **Fixed and verified** (on Windows, against PostgreSQL 18.6, with the checks below): G2-01 to G2-08, the small gaps A to E, and F-1 to F-5.
- **Linux evidence obtained** (CI, the run for `9eaa152`, 11 October 2026): the worker's real `SIGTERM` stop with a message in flight (OB-24; the first run's failure was a defect of the test), the capture sink's file and folder mode tests, the API's `SIGTERM` stop, the idle worker's and the other tests Windows skips. OB-25 and OB-26 are for their own tasks.
- **Approved for a later consumer:** P-1 (S0.34, S0.49, and the bootstrap task S0.30 for the seed's audit authority).
- **Awaiting the owner:** P-3 (provisional), OB-100 and P-4 before S0.20, OB-113 and OB-114 (which identity may delete, and the retention of a finished job's metadata), OB-115, the event for `AC-SLICE-000-93`, and whether `REDEMPTION_UNCONFIRMED` should stay a deferral with no count cap (it is, because a reader that is down for a day must not destroy a live message; a cap by count would be a product value).
- **Release-only:** the deployment check that every serving instance holds a new limiter key version before it is written under (G2-04, step 2), and its retirement discipline; the qualification of the engineering figures (the deferral waits, the limiter's capacity and throughput) at S0.65; the signed checkpoint chain (G1); the real provider (G3).

**The Linux and CI record.** GitHub Actions exists for this repository (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`): its jobs run on `ubuntu-24.04` (static checks; unit, component and process tests; database tests against a service container; build, contract and browser tests; the dependency audit; and the aggregate `CI result`). The bootstrap passed there on Linux at `main` `9c34b69` (the owner's record), which covers none of the Slice-000 code written since.

**The first hosted run of this tree** is the pull request run for `955c6ef` (run 38131713808, 11 October 2026). It passed the static checks, the unit, component and process tests (which run the Linux-only tests that Windows skips, among them the real-`SIGTERM` stops of the API and of the idle worker), the build, contract and browser tests and the dependency audit. It **failed the database job on one test and no other**: `delivery-worker-process.db.test.ts`, "does the same after a real SIGTERM" (OB-24), which found `UNKNOWN` where `RELINQUISHED` was expected. The defect was the test's, not the worker's. It waited for the worker's log line for the claim, which is written at debug and so never at the default level, behind a `catch` that swallowed the timeout, and so signalled about eight seconds after the claim, after the send's own five seconds had run out. A send whose time ran out is recorded `UNKNOWN`, as G2-02 requires, because the message may have been taken. On the real signal the process itself ended with exit code 0, no signal and an empty stderr.

Reproduced on Windows with the fixture's own timer before the change (a stop eight seconds after the claim gives `UNKNOWN`, one about a second after gives `RELINQUISHED`) and corrected: the test fixture writes one line as the provider receives the message, the test waits for that line and signals at once under a 3 s shutdown budget (so the signal may be late by 3.5 s before the send's time is spent), and the test makes the same assertions as the self-signal test, which it now shares. The correction was run on Windows only, where this test is skipped. **The pull request run for the correction (`9eaa152`, run 38136618454) passed every job**, the database job and this test among them, which obtains the Linux evidence of OB-24; whether Gate 2 passes is the owner's decision, on the conditions below. Neither Git Bash nor WSL was needed.
