**Not run, and why.** `pnpm test` as one command at the default concurrency (its parts ran: `test:root`, and every package's `test` at concurrency 1); `api-client:check` and `test:browser` (no contract, client or browser behaviour changed); `pnpm audit` (no dependency changed); every Linux run. **Skipped, not passed:** the Windows-skipped tests as before (API 8, `test:db` 2, root 2, harness 1).

**The "Timeout waiting for worker to respond" investigation (unresolved).**

- *The command.* `pnpm test` is `pnpm run test:root && turbo run test` (turbo 2.11.7, default concurrency), so the `test` task of four packages runs at once (turbo counts five tasks): `@melarc/e2e` (the node:test harness), `@melarc/api` (vitest, projects `unit` and `process`; the second forks the API and the worker as child processes), `@melarc/api-client` (vitest), `@melarc/ops-web` (vitest 5.0.3, projects `components` on jsdom and `node`).
- *The settings.* `apps/ops-web/vitest.config.ts` sets `testTimeout` and `hookTimeout` to 20 s and names no pool or worker count, so vitest's defaults apply (a forks pool, up to the machine's parallelism). The message is vitest's own: a forked worker must report that it has started within 60 s (`START_TIMEOUT` in vitest's pool; the project cannot set it).
- *The output.* In run 1, 11 of 23 Ops files: "[vitest-pool]: Failed to start forks worker for test files ... Caused by: [vitest-pool-runner]: Timeout waiting for worker to respond". The two earlier occurrences (11 of 23 files each, passing on an unchanged rerun) were not sampled.
- *The timing.* The Ops suite run alone on the same machine takes 17.8 s for all 23 files, so a start that takes 60 s is not the suite's own cost.
- *The resource evidence.* A read-only sampler (every 2 s: CPU, free and committed memory, the number and memory of `node` processes) ran through all three `pnpm test` runs of this section. Run 1, Ops failed: CPU 100% for 23 samples, **free memory down to 245 MB** of 15.9 GB, 81% committed, 39 `node` processes, 3.98 GB of `node` memory. Run 2, Ops passed: CPU 100% for 26 samples, free memory no lower than 1047 MB, 78% committed, 29 processes, 3.66 GB. Run 3, Ops passed: CPU 100% for 23 samples, free memory no lower than 2150 MB, 78% committed, 40 processes, 3.54 GB. The machine is a 6-core, 12-thread workstation with 15.9 GB, shared with 26 to 35 Chrome processes and the Docker VM (about 0.7 GB). The unsigned process of the earlier machine finding was not running.
- *What this shows, and what it does not.* The run that failed is the run in which free memory fell to a tenth of a gigabyte; CPU saturation and the number of processes do not separate the runs. That is a correlation in three sampled runs, one of them a failure. It is not a cause, it was not reproduced on demand, and the sampler adds a small load of its own.
- *Bounded comparison (follow-up, 11 October 2026).* One run, chosen in advance, with everything enabled: `pnpm run test:root` (the first half of `pnpm test`; it ran just before, exit 0) and then `TURBO_FORCE=true pnpm exec turbo run test --concurrency=1` in place of Turbo's default concurrency, so that the four packages' suites ran one after another and not together. No timeout was raised, nothing was left out, the Ops pool, the CI workflow and the repository's scripts were left as they were, and the run was not repeated. *Result:* exit 0 in 3 min 16 s, five tasks successful and none cached: Ops Portal 23 files and 220 tests (15.6 s), API 144 files with 4469 passed and 8 skipped (132.7 s), api-client 250, harness 199 passed and 1 skipped; **no worker failed to start**. The same read-only sampler recorded the 87 samples of the run: CPU at 100% in 15 (23 to 26 at the default concurrency), free memory no lower than 1080 MB (245 MB in the failing run; 1047 MB and 2150 MB in the passing ones), at most 78% committed, **at most 15 `node` processes and 2.4 GB of `node` memory (29 to 40 processes and 3.5 to 4.0 GB at the default)**, 26 to 31 Chrome processes and the Docker VM at 961 MB. *What it shows:* with the suites serialised the `node` footprint was less than half, and the Ops Portal started every file. *What it does not:* the Ops Portal also started every file in two of the three default-concurrency runs, a 1080 MB floor cannot be told from the 1047 MB of a passing default run, and one passing run neither reproduces the failure nor refutes it. It is consistent with contention among the concurrent suites and is not a cause.
- *Disposition.* **Unresolved.** No timeout was raised, no test disabled, no worker count or concurrency changed, and the suite was not repeated until it passed (the reruns above were needed for the two other failures). Two bounded resource-management changes would fit the evidence and are **not applied**, because the evidence does not establish the cause: capping turbo's concurrency for `test`, or capping the Ops pool's `maxWorkers`. The owner decides, and such a change would be recorded as a resource-management change with every suite still run. The older timing failures of `execution-budget.db.test.ts` (OB-91) are a separate matter that this evidence does not explain: that file passed in both `test:db` runs. **After the follow-up's comparison the matter is still unresolved**: nothing permanent was changed, CI's concurrency is untouched, and a lower-concurrency `test` script or a cap on the Ops pool remains the owner's decision with this evidence, at a cost in wall-clock time to every run.

**Gate 2 status.** Not passed and not claimed. Evidence reached: *fixture/platform verified* on Windows (a real PostgreSQL 18.6, the technical logins, the test kit), including the follow-up, **and on Linux in CI**: the pull request run for `9eaa152` (11 October 2026, `ubuntu-24.04`) passed every job, which obtains the Linux evidence of OB-24 (the real-`SIGTERM` stops of the API and of the worker, idle and with a message in flight) and OB-62. No route, screen or contract operation exists, and the contract comparison of zero operations proves nothing about identity conformance. Still owed: (1) the release-procedure check that every instance holds a new limiter key version before it is written under (G2-04); (2) S0.65's qualification of the engineering figures (the deferral waits, the limiter's capacity and throughput); (3) the owner's decisions: P-3 (provisional), P-4 and OB-100 before S0.20, OB-113, OB-114, OB-115 and the event for `AC-SLICE-000-93`; (4) the work approved for later consumers: P-1 (S0.34, S0.49, and the seed's audit authority before S0.30). Resolved by the follow-up: F-1 to F-5, P-2, P-5, P-6 and P-7 (OB-102 and OB-111). The checkpoint commit and its correction are on the pull request branch and the latest run passed every job; whether Gate 2 passes is the owner's call.

### 7.16 Carried from S0.20 to S0.24 (batch of 11 October 2026)

**Authorization and scope.** The owner's instruction of 11 October 2026 authorized S0.20 to S0.24 as **one** bounded batch on a new branch for Gate 3, `feat/slice-000-gate-3` (created at `0cb2d6d`), with no S0.25 or later task and no push, merge or deployment. The batch is built and left **uncommitted, unstaged and unpushed** on that branch. (A later message of the owner named "S0.20 to S0.23"; the original authorization, which includes S0.24, was followed, and S0.24 is built. If S0.24 was not meant, it is a separate set of paths: `migrations/0016_*`, `platform/auth/sessions/`, `src/staff/`, the unit of work, the authentication guard and their tests.) **Gate 3 is not passed and is not claimed:** S0.25 to S0.27 are not started, and S0.23's proofs that need a test CA are blocked (below).

**Owner decisions taken during the batch (11 October 2026).**

- **OB-55, answered:** a commit whose outcome is unknown (`CommitOutcomeUnknownError`) is `500` with no code, no `Retry-After` and the fixed text "The outcome of the request could not be confirmed"; the platform's operational failures are a code-less `503`; a definite rollback is an ordinary `500`. No error code was added and the contract is unchanged.
- **OB-100 / P-4 (a command in progress under its key): left open** until the S0.26 fixture operation. No mapping was built; the idempotency stage is S0.26's.
- **S0.23 without a CA.** The owner did not resolve the 7 October machine finding, so no CA or key was generated on this machine and no CI change was made. S0.23 was built as far as it needs no private key; every proof that does is reported **blocked**, with the command for a clean machine or CI.

**What was implemented and verified.** Each task below was built test first and then held to deliberate breaks of its own code (the "Break caught" comments); the final uncached gate is at the end of this section.

- **S0.20, transport admission** (`platform/http/admission/`, `create-app.ts`; OB-37 pipeline half, OB-55, OB-112). Head-stage refusals before routing (`415` for an encoded body, a media type that is not `application/json` or a charset that is not UTF-8; `413` for a declared or counted length over `HTTP_BODY_LIMIT_BYTES`, counted as the bytes arrive, chunked bodies included), the admission gate (a bounded concurrency, a line as long as the concurrency, a waiting deadline; the probes are exempt by their exact paths), the body deadline (`408`), Node's own header and request deadlines made real by the connection check interval, and a request `ExecutionBudget` created once a request is admitted and its body received, set with `runWithBudget` and finished when the response is over, with the pipeline's own `503` after `DISPOSAL_BOUND_MS` for work that heeds nothing. A body that is not valid JSON, or that names `__proto__`, is carried as a parse-failure marker to the validation step. `fingerprintOfRequest` cannot omit the path parameters (OB-112). Real-HTTP and real-PostgreSQL proofs: `transport-admission.test.ts`, `admission-gate.test.ts`, `request-budget.test.ts`, `request-pipeline.db.test.ts`, `operational-failure.test.ts`.
- **S0.21, HTTP primitives and the description composition** (`platform/http/error-codes.ts`, `platform/auth/cookies/cookie-writer.ts`, `platform/openapi/operation-statements.ts`). The 121 error codes of the contract and their statuses (`statusOf`; the codes the axis table does not give a status are `422`, and `SETTING_MISSING` and `SETTING_INVALID` have none decided), `ApiException` setting only the headers the contract declares on an error, the cookie writer (`Max-Age=0` literally on expiry, the vendor device cookie never expired, real repeated `Set-Cookie` lines), and the description statements that a decorated class cannot make (`DescribeSecurity`, `DescribeCookies`, `DescribeServers`, the default posture), compared with the contract and never copied from it, with drift tests in `check-application.test.ts`.
- **S0.22, access declarations and the guard chain** (`platform/routes/`). The four declarations (`technical`, `anonymous` with its bucket and identifier, `self`, `permission`), each stating the same fact in the description (`AC-SLICE-000-25` and `-43` are *fixture/platform verified*: `guard-chain.test.ts`, `access-declaration.test.ts`, `anonymity-contract.test.ts`, `guard-chain.db.test.ts`), the chain in the refusal order (declaration, rate limit, authentication, CSRF and Origin, authorization) with the rate-limit guard real over the PostgreSQL limiter, which the runtime composition now binds where it can be built, and the later slots failing closed. Break checks of the guards found gaps (a second bucket, the unsafe methods other than `POST`, a technical write, the authorization slot, a four-segment key) and one redundant line; the tests that close them, and the removal of the line, are in the tree.
- **S0.24, sessions and the authentication guard** (migration `0016`, `platform/auth/sessions/`, `src/staff/`, `PostgresUnitOfWork`). The `session` and a minimal `staff_identity` table with row-level security, column-level grants and guard triggers (a session's token, authority and expiry never change and an ended session stays ended; an identity's origin is immutable; a vendor account and a rider have one live session at the database); the session store (lookup by token hash, coalesced activity, ending by session, principal or bundle with `belowRevision`); the authenticator and the authentication guard (cookie for staff and vendor, bearer for rider, never both and never the wrong one; one fresh database time; equality is expired; a rider has no idle rule; undifferentiated `SESSION_INVALID`, `SESSION_SUPERSEDED` for a displaced vendor only; the authenticated-API bucket applied to the session just found); the session-issue protocol of SECURITY_DESIGN.md §13.10a on the S0.7 toolkit (discover, plan, lock, take the identity lock, revalidate, restart at most three times, then copy the bundle, take one database time, issue and audit in one transaction); `contextOfSession` (OB-54); the real unit of work and a rule that only it and the coordinator make a handle (OB-99); the staff `IdentityDirectory`; the persona factory for tests (`test/database/support/personas.ts`). The issue protocol's evidence (OB-11) ran on real rows under the runtime login: a session records the locked bundle's content and revision, the shared policy lock is held while it is written, an identity reassigned (bundle, status, credential or hubs) between discovery and lock restarts within the bound with nothing written before the revalidation passed, and the restarts end in the generic failure.
- **S0.23, the trusted HTTPS harness: partial, built without a CA.** `e2e/harness/tls-material.ts` (the policy for a directory of test certificates: **outside the checkout wherever the path leads** — a link, a junction, an extended-length or share spelling is resolved and compared by the identity of the folders, and the path as written counts too —, a self-signed CA that carries **critical name constraints**, read from the certificate's own structure, a leaf that names the loopback and was signed by that CA, both valid for at most 31 days, the leaf's key readable by its owner alone), `test-ca.ts` and `pnpm --filter @melarc/e2e run tls:generate` (the generator, tested against a fake `openssl`, **never run**: it keeps the CA's serial file in its work directory, checks that exactly the three files remain, and on any failure removes the folders it made or empties the directory it was given), and the evidence policy of the specs that show a secret (`evidence-policy.ts`, `secret-test.ts`, `secret-evidence.ts`: a project of their own with the recorders off, a fixture that checks the resolved options, the page snapshot of Playwright turned off, a check of the syntax of every spec at any depth, and a browser proof on what Playwright writes: §7.17). **Blocked, reported and not green:** the per-engine trust spike (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit, branded Chrome and Edge), the HTTPS Ops origin in the stack, and every proof that needs a `Secure` cookie over HTTPS. The engine matrix actually run is Chromium over plain HTTP, as before. Nothing in `ci.yml`, `ci-workflow.ts` or `REQUIRED_COMMANDS` changed.

**Engineering parameters and their qualification.** All **development-qualified and not production-qualified**; S0.65 calibrates them. S0.20's (the receipt limits, the admission gate, the budget, the answer grace) and S0.24's (`ACTIVITY_COALESCE_MS` = 30 seconds, the five-minute future bound on a recorded activity, the restart bound, the issue deadline) are tabled in [DEPLOYMENT_AND_ENVIRONMENTS.md](../../architecture/DEPLOYMENT_AND_ENVIRONMENTS.md) §12.5, "What S0.20 applied" and "What S0.24 applied". S0.21 and S0.22 add none. S0.23 adds two for test material: certificates are valid for at most 31 days (`MAX_VALID_DAYS`) and a generated pair for seven unless told otherwise.

**Independent review of the batch (11 October 2026) and what it changed.** One read-only reviewer with a fresh context read the S0.24 code and the S0.23 harness (it could run unit tests and the migration lint, not the database tests). Nothing it reported was taken on trust: each finding was checked against the code and the documents and, where it held, fixed test first, the new test being seen to fail for the stated reason before the change.
- *High, not a defect of the tree:* "`session_guard` is created but never attached". The reviewer read the migration while one of my break checks was running on it (a check removes a line and restores the file by hash); the migration of the tree creates both triggers (`session_guard` and `staff_identity_guard`), and `session-schema.db.test.ts` proves them on a migrated database. The rule that follows is kept: no break check runs while a reviewer reads. Its secondary point held: the guard let `last_activity_at` jump to any future time, which disables the idle limit (`judgeSession` treats it as not idle). The trigger now refuses an activity more than five minutes after the database's own clock, and one that moves back.
- *Medium, held:* the issuer could not tell which credential the caller verified, so a session could be issued on a credential that was replaced after the verification and then outlive the replacement. `IssueSessionRequest.verifiedCredentialAt` was compared under the identity lock with the moment the identity's credential was established (`CREDENTIAL_CHANGED`); the audit that followed (§7.17, F02) showed that a moment cannot tell two credentials apart, and it is now the credential's generation (`verifiedCredentialGeneration`). A replacement between discovery and lock restarts and then refuses; a request that states none, or one that is not a whole number of one or more, is a `TypeError`. What the writers of a credential must do for this to hold is OB-128.
- *Low, held, fixed test first:* two `Authorization` header lines are merged by Node and the first used, so two bearer credentials were not refused as ambiguous like two session cookies (now judged from the raw header lines); the schema allowed a browser session with no CSRF hash (`session_csrf_by_principal`: a hash exactly when the session is not a rider's); `terminate` raised `23514` and rolled the whole revoking transaction back after the database clock stepped back past a session's issue time (`greatest(clock_timestamp(), issued_at)`); the test-CA's "outside the checkout" check compared text, and the reviewer showed it accepting a junction into the checkout, `\\?\E:\…` and `\\localhost\E$\…` (all three are refused now, wherever the path leads; a read-only probe on this machine confirmed the extended-length, case-different, `\\localhost\E$` and `\\127.0.0.1\E$` spellings are refused); the generator left `ca.srl` in the result directory by OpenSSL's default (`-CAserial` keeps it in the work directory), removed only the three known files on a failure, and its policy checked no name constraints (see S0.23 above).
- *Low, reported and not changed:* that activity is recorded before a later step refuses the request (finding 13); that the floor checks would land after CSRF (OB-130); and that a staff context can read its own `credential_hash` (finding 14).
- *Checked and sound, as the reviewer found:* the lock order policy, then identity, then session, and the bundle lock held across the read and the insert; nothing written before the revalidation passes; coalesced activity only moves forward; expiry and idleness expire at equality and every judged time is a millisecond `Date`; cookie and bearer kept apart, and no token in a log, an event or a parameter; fail-closed composition; each policy a single `CASE` with a closed `ELSE`, narrower than the registry.

**Findings and decisions for the Product Owner** (nothing here was defaulted or invented):

1. **Where the authenticated-API bucket sits in the refusal order.** permission-enforcement.md §5.1 puts "rate limit" first, before authentication; the authenticated bucket is keyed on the *active session*, which exists only once the credential has been verified, and a limiter keyed on an unverified credential lets any caller invent keys and fill `rate_limit_admission` (OB-110). It is applied by the authentication guard, right after the session is found and before CSRF, authorization and validation. The pre-authentication buckets are keyed from the body at step 1, as written. The owner confirms or changes the wording of §5.1.
2. **`SETTING_MISSING` and `SETTING_INVALID` have no decided HTTP status** (`ERROR_STATUS` holds `null`); a setting that is missing or invalid is an unexpected `500` today. *(Decided by the owner on 12 October 2026, decision B, and built: the existing fixed, code-less `503`; [§7.18](#718-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026).)*
3. **Forty-one status inconsistencies in operations outside the thirty-eight of this slice** (statuses declared in the contract that disagree with the axis table's rule, errors-and-enums.md §4). They are later slices' and untouched; each slice that builds one of those operations resolves its own.
4. **`Expect: 100-continue` is not handled**, and a refusal that keeps the connection open drains the rest of the body, bounded by the request deadline; a slot is given back when the response is over, so a handler that goes on after its client has gone is bounded by its budget only.
5. **The limiter is composed without a metrics registry**, so `rate_limited_total` is not counted until S0.68 (OB-107).
6. **Two behaviours changed on purpose:** a body that is not `application/json` is now refused `415` at the transport, and a small malformed JSON body is answered at the validation step, after the guards, as the refusal order requires (`AC-SLICE-000-130`).
7. **The minimal `staff_identity` carries `credential_hash` and `credential_established_at`**, because S0.31 (which lists no migration) writes the credential and S0.32 adds only the failed-attempt count and lock time; (and, since §7.17, `credential_generation`), and the runtime holds `UPDATE` on those three columns **now, before anything writes them**, because a row lock (`FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, the identity lock of §13.10a) needs an `UPDATE` privilege on at least one column. The registry allows exactly this for the `authentication` capability ("`UPDATE` of the credential record only"). The owner confirms.
8. **`session.state` is not a column:** a session is active while `terminated_at` is null, so there is no second source of truth. The port's `NewSession.surface` was removed (it is derived from the principal type) and `PrincipalSummary` gained `bundleId` and `authorizedHubIds` and `IdentityDirectory` gained `readIdentity` (the non-authoritative discovery read).
9. **`SessionStore.terminate` by bundle takes `belowRevision`**, the filter a reduction needs (S0.27 calls it); the browser session's `csrf_token_hash` is created by the issuer with the session (S0.25 validates against it).
10. **The own-clock rule now flags `new Date()` with no argument**, not every `new Date(...)`: a date built from a value reads no clock, and the database's `timestamptz` text is read through `timestampOf`.
11. *(Built in S0.26 and S0.27: [§7.18](#718-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026).)* **The request-time authority checks are S0.26's and S0.27's, not S0.24's** (a below-floor session is still accepted by authentication, and nothing is served yet because authorization refuses every route that needs a principal). The same holds for the lock rule of a state-changing request: authentication judges the session once, in its lookup, which takes no policy lock (a read-only request takes none, §13.10a); the shared policy lock on the acting session's bundle and the re-check of the session under it are S0.26's.
12. **Mutation checks.** The owner asked for no broad mutation campaign. Break checks were run on the **new code of this batch only** (the guards of S0.22, the sessions code, the migration, S0.21's earlier set, and the changes the review caused), each mutant restored by hash, about 175 mutants in all; the survivors were either equivalent (one, the order in which a bearer and a cookie are returned after the ambiguity check) or closed by a test. If that is more than the instruction meant, say so.
13. *(Decided by the owner on 12 October 2026, decision A, and built in S0.26: [§7.18](#718-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026).)* **Any request that authentication accepts counts as activity, even one a later step refuses.** The activity is recorded (coalesced) in the authentication step, before the authenticated-API bucket, CSRF and authorization run, so a request answered `429` or `403` still extends `last_activity_at`, and a screen that polls an endpoint it is refused keeps a session alive. `authentication-guard.db.test.ts` states the behaviour. Whether a refused request should count is a product decision; until it is made, the Ops Portal must not poll while it is unattended (S0.38 onward).
14. **A staff context could read its own `credential_hash` (closed in §7.17, F04; kept here as the record of the finding and of the choice the owner still has).** `staff_identity_select` admits a staff member's own row and the runtime login holds a table-wide `SELECT` (one login serves the staff context and the `authentication` capability, so a column grant cannot tell them apart). The registry allows self rows, and nothing reads the column for a staff context, but domain-model.md §6.8 says a credential is never retrievable, and only the absence of a defect in a handler keeps it so. A table of its own for the credential, whose policy admits the `authentication` capability alone, is one fix: a classification in the registry and a migration. The fix made (§7.17) is narrower and needs neither: the runtime has no `SELECT` on the column at all, and the `authentication` capability reads it through one `SECURITY DEFINER` function that refuses every other context. The owner may still prefer the table (it leaves the identity row with no column the runtime may update for the identity lock, which is why it was not taken).

**Gate of the batch** (uncached, `TURBO_FORCE=true`, Node 24.21.0, pnpm 11.1.3, Windows 11, a local PostgreSQL 18.6; run once, on the tree handed over, after the review's fixes: `0cb2d6d` plus 45 modified and 74 new paths, unstaged, unchanged from the start of the gate to its end). `format:check`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `build`, `contract:check` (0 operations compared: the machinery only), `api-client:check` and `db:check` exit 0; `test:db` exit 0 (1198 passed and 2 skipped in 43 files; `execution-budget.db.test.ts` 15 passed); `test:e2e` exit 0 (16 passed). **`pnpm test` exited 1 once** and exit 0 on one repeat of the same command, alone and uncached, on the unchanged tree (3 min 27 s, five of five tasks, none cached): root 1191 tests (1189 passed, 2 skipped), API 161 files (4918 passed, 8 skipped), Ops Portal 23 files (220 passed), api-client 9 files (250 passed), harness 14 files (277 passed, 2 skipped).

- *The failure.* In the first run 11 of the 23 Ops Portal files did not start ("[vitest-pool-runner]: Timeout waiting for worker to respond"; the 12 that started passed, 143 tests), and turbo stopped the API run before it had reported, so the API counts above are the repeat's. It is the signature of the earlier occurrences ([§7.15](#715-gate-2-audit-corrections-11-october-2026-disposition)), in a package this batch did not touch, and **it is not the corrected worker `SIGTERM` test**, which is a different suite and was not involved. Nothing was raised, disabled or reconfigured, and the command was run once more and not again.
- *What was captured.* The failing run was not sampled. The repeat and the `test:db` and `test:e2e` runs were, by the read-only sampler of §7.15 (every 2 seconds, changing nothing). The repeat, which passed: CPU at 100% in 33 of 101 samples (mean 58%), free memory no lower than 1165 MB, at most 88% committed, at most 44 `node` processes and 3.6 GB of `node` memory, 33 Chrome processes: the profile of the passing default-concurrency runs of §7.15 (29 to 40 processes, 3.5 to 4.0 GB). That profile does not separate a passing run from a failing one, so **the cause is not established**; it is consistent with contention among the four suites that turbo runs at once and is not shown to be it. Capping turbo's concurrency for `test` or the Ops pool's `maxWorkers` remains the owner's decision, with this evidence, at a cost in wall-clock time to every run.
- *`execution-budget.db.test.ts` did not fail* in this gate, so there was no failure to capture (its 15 tests passed within `test:db`). Over `test:db` (the sampler was started 40 seconds into its 8 minutes) it saw CPU below 99% throughout (mean 32%) and free memory no lower than 2536 MB. Its cause stays unexplained (OB-91) and is not attributed to machine load.

**Not run, and why.** `test:browser` (no browser behaviour changed); `pnpm audit` (no dependency changed: the lockfile and every dependency list are as at `0cb2d6d`, and the one `package.json` change is the script `tls:generate` in `e2e/package.json`); every Linux, WSL2 and CI check; every proof of S0.23 that needs a test CA; `melarc_dev` was not touched. **Skipped, not passed:** the Windows-skipped tests as before (API 8, `test:db` 2, root 2) and, in the harness, the POSIX-mode test of the generator (2 skipped, one more than before). **Tooling:** Graphify 0.9.85 (`graphifyy`, installed with pip, not editable, from the local clone of Graphify-Labs/graphify at `v0.9.85`, commit `b4e6307`) was used for orientation only, as `graphify update .` after code changes (code and document headings by syntax tree; no semantic pass, no model or API key; `post-commit` and `post-checkout` hooks and the Claude Code hook-guard are local); its output is local, git-ignored and not evidence.

**Gate 3 status.** Not passed and not claimed: S0.25 to S0.27 are not started, S0.23's CA-dependent proofs are blocked, the Linux run of this tree has not happened, and the decisions above are the owner's. Evidence reached by this batch: *fixture/platform verified* on Windows.

### 7.17 Corrections after the independent audit of 11 October 2026

**What this is.** An independent audit of the source package of §7.16 (`melarc-s0.20-s0.24-source.zip`, SHA-256 `eb15edb9783990205255d93d567e625a150f202c0c06948f88563ea215be51db`) reported seven findings (F01 to F07), one concurrency obligation, recommendations on the owner's open choices and remarks on the test process. It read source and ran isolated probes; it did not run the database, browser or end-to-end suites. Nothing in it was taken on trust: each finding was checked against the code and, where it could be, reproduced (F01 against a real PostgreSQL, F05 over sockets, F06 on what Playwright writes) before it was fixed, test first, and the new code was held to deliberate breaks, restored by hash. **This section is the record; there is no separate handoff.** Nothing is committed, staged or pushed, and Gate 3 is not claimed.

| Finding| Verdict| What changed| Evidence|
|---|---|---|---|
| F01 High: an open transaction can issue statements and commit after its request is finished| **Confirmed in source, and reproduced against PostgreSQL** (the three tests of a finished request failed before the fix)| The statement guard of `DatabaseService.transactionWithContext` (the exclusive handle shares it) refuses a closed ancestor as well as an expired budget; a closed request is rolled back instead of committed, with a warning; the caller is given `BudgetClosedError`, not the failed query that carries it. `CommitOutcomeUnknownError` is untouched: a commit already sent is never called a rollback| `execution-budget.db.test.ts` (a request finished mid-transaction, after the last statement, on the exclusive handle; a control that commits), `request-pipeline.db.test.ts` (a real client that leaves while its transaction waits: the late write is refused and nothing commits). Three breaks caught; the fourth, a check at the start of the transaction's body, is unreachable (the child budget's constructor refuses a closed parent in the same tick) and is kept as a statement of intent|
| F02 High: a timestamp is not a credential generation identifier| **Confirmed.** Two database values 0.8 ms apart read into the same JavaScript date| `staff_identity.credential_generation` (zero while there is no credential), a trigger that holds it (rises by exactly one and never moves back; `credential_established_at` changes only with a new generation and may repeat; a rehash changes neither, though the database cannot tell a replacement from a rehash by the hash string), the port (`PrincipalSummary.credentialGeneration`), the issuer (`verifiedCredentialGeneration`, compared under the identity lock). OB-128 rewritten| `session-schema.db.test.ts` (the tests of the generation rules, five of the boundary), `session-issue.db.test.ts` (replacement within one millisecond, at an earlier moment, a rehash only, between discovery and lock, a replacement held off by the identity lock until the issuing transaction commits, malformed requests). Ten breaks of the migration and six of the issuer, all caught except one redundant check, removed|
| F03 High, prerequisite: session issuance cannot join the whole sign-in transaction| **Confirmed as an interface limitation** (no live exploit)| The issue is the sign-in's one transaction owner: under the identity lock, after every refusal, it runs the request's typed `steps` (writes of identity proofs, at most four; database work by convention and review, not a sandbox), ends a Vendor's or a Rider's earlier session with the reason of its audience and records it (`auth.session.superseded`; `auth.session.terminated`), then inserts the session. `SessionStore.terminate` now returns the ids it ended. OB-129 rewritten| `session-signin.db.test.ts` (16 tests, with fixture identities locked by an advisory lock): replacement for a vendor and a rider, none for staff, a failed insert and a failed audit leave the earlier session and write nothing, three concurrent sign-ins end with one live session, a proof spent with the session and unspent when the issue fails, one proof for two racing sign-ins, no step for a refused principal. Eleven breaks, all caught|
| F04 Medium: credential hashes share a broad read boundary| **Confirmed** (and in the batch's own report)| The runtime login has no `SELECT` on `credential_hash` (a column list on every other column), so no context under it can select it or say `*` (the owner role and the migration login can read what they own: a boundary between application roles); `UPDATE` on the three credential columns stays (the identity lock needs it). `melarc.staff_credential_hash(uuid)` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, owner role, fixed search path, `EXECUTE` for the runtime alone, listed in `APPROVED_DEFINER_EXECUTE`) returns it to a `SYSTEM` context under `authentication` and raises `42501` for any other. SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.9| `session-schema.db.test.ts`: every context refused on the column, on `*`, in an expression; the function refused for staff, vendor, rider and another capability and answering the capability; its owner, search path and `EXECUTE` list; the catalogue and registry-grant suites pass unchanged. A table of its own remains the owner's option (finding 14)|
| F05 Medium: transport exemptions and refused-body handling leave capacity gaps| **Confirmed** (three paths)| The probes are exempt as probes: `GET` or `HEAD` of the exact path with no body (`probeRequests`), for the gate and the budget. `Expect: 100-continue` is no longer answered by Node at the head: the invitation is written when the head is accepted and a slot is held, never to a refused request or one still in the line. A refusal that leaves the connection open reads off the rest within bounds (`refused-body.ts`: sixteen times the body limit, the body deadline, the admission concurrency, no new setting); beyond them the connection is closed after the answer, and with no room the refusal says `Connection: close`| `transport-bounds.test.ts` (17 tests over sockets). The first allowance (the body limit itself) was caught by an existing test as a reset under a `fetch` of a body twice the limit and raised to sixteen times. Twelve breaks, one equivalent on loopback (the order of the cut and the flush)|
| F06 Medium: the evidence guard proves text presence, not that recording is off| **Confirmed (the three bypass forms), and a worse leak found: Playwright 1.63 writes a text snapshot of the page into `error-context.md` for a failing test with all three recorders off** (the sentinel was in the file)| A project of its own, `chromium-secret`, for specs named `<name>.secret.test.ts`, with the recorders off; the ordinary project does not match them. An automatic fixture in `secret-test.ts` fails the test before it begins if a recorder is on after every `test.use`. `PLAYWRIGHT_NO_COPY_PROMPT` is set by the configuration for the runner and every worker. The check of the sources reads syntax (not text) of every file under `tests/` at any depth. A canary spec keeps the project from being empty (the test inventory refuses an empty one). `evidence-policy.ts`| `secret-evidence.test.ts` (the three bypasses, thirteen forbidden forms, mentions that are not calls, the real configuration's shape, nested discovery); `tests/evidence-policy.spec.ts`, a browser proof on what Playwright writes: a failing synthetic spec under the real project definitions leaves no recording and no sentinel, an override does not start, an ordinary failing spec keeps its pictures (the control) but not the page as text, and Playwright left alone does write it. Fifteen breaks caught|
| F07 Medium: critical name constraints are not proof of loopback-only constraints| **Confirmed** (the claim was wider than the policy)| The claim is narrowed to what the policy reads (the constraints exist and bind), the CA must verify under its own key and not only say it is self-signed, and the leaf must name the loopback and nothing else. The handwritten reader is not grown into a validator. The proof that the CA refuses a name outside the loopback is `openssl verify` where the CA is made (commands in `e2e/README.md`), owed on a clean machine or in CI| `tls-material.test.ts`; three breaks caught. A read-only probe on this machine confirmed an extended-length path, a different case and the administrative share of the local disk are refused as the checkout|

**Found while correcting (not in the audit).** (1) The page snapshot of Playwright (F06 above). (2) The test inventory refuses a Playwright project whose `testMatch` matches no spec and a `testIgnore` it does not model, so the secret project is named by two non-overlapping string literals (`**/*.spec.ts`, `**/*.secret.test.ts`) and has a canary. (3) A stale `apps/api/dist` makes the API of the end-to-end stack not ready after the migration changes (the posture check finds an `EXECUTE` the old build does not list): `pnpm run test:e2e` builds first, a direct `playwright test` does not. (4) The earlier edit of this plan's gate paragraph said that `pnpm test` was cancelled; turbo stopped the API run before it reported.

**The owner's choices (the audit's recommendations, which are recommendations and not decisions).**

|---|---|---|

| Missing or invalid required settings| A code-less `503`; detail internal and redacted; no undocumented client code| **Not changed**: `SETTING_MISSING` and `SETTING_INVALID` still have no decided status. Recommended, awaiting the owner|

Where in the refusal order it sits is OB-130 (the authentication step, not S0.26's slot after CSRF), awaiting the owner|

Today any request that authentication accepts counts|

| S0.23 with no CA on this machine| Keep the restriction; finish the trust evidence in a cleared environment| Kept|
| S0.24 scope| No rollback for the shorter title of the pasted batch; review as supplied and correct| Followed|

**The test process.** The Ops Portal worker-start timeout is unresolved test infrastructure, and a passing repeat does not explain a failed first run; §7.16 says so and keeps the failing run unsampled. The audit recommends a deliberate workstation resource policy: cap the concurrent package runs and/or the worker count, then measure the normal command, preserving every test and assertion and increasing no timeout, with hosted CI's limits justified separately. **Not applied**: it changes the `test` script (CI runs it) and is the owner's. A concrete proposal, with the earlier measurement behind it (§7.15: `turbo run test --concurrency=1` started every Ops file in a run that the default concurrency failed in one of three): a local `test` command with `--concurrency=2`, CI untouched. No mutation campaign was run beyond the new code of each correction (about 60 breaks), and the affected suites were run once on the final tree followed by the one consolidated gate below.

**Graphify** is an optional navigation index and not evidence of security, transaction correctness or coverage; nothing in a build or a test depends on it, and the audit's remark that its hooks and installation were not in the archive is right (they are local and ignored).

**Gate of the corrections** (uncached, `TURBO_FORCE=true`, Node 24.21.0, pnpm 11.1.3, Windows 11, a local PostgreSQL 18.6; `0cb2d6d` plus 132 changed paths (51 modified, 81 new), unstaged). It was run, and then repeated where it did not end in a clean exit, and **every failure in it but one was a mistake of mine, not of the code**; each is recorded because the evidence is only as good as its history.

| Run| Result| Cause, and what was done|
|---|---|---|
| First, after the corrections| `format:check`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `build`, `contract:check`, `api-client:check`, `db:check` exit 0| (`build` and the three checks ran once: nothing they read changed afterwards)|
| | `pnpm test` exit 1: 11 of 23 Ops Portal files did not start ("Timeout waiting for worker to respond"), 12 passed (143 tests), turbo stopped the API run| The known, unresolved Ops worker-start timeout, the second default-concurrency run of the day to fail it. **This one was sampled** (below)|
| | `test:db` exit 1: 1 of 1238 failed (`seed-initialization.db.test.ts`, "are the whole of the allow-list")| Mine: the test pins which logins may execute a definer function, and I had added the runtime's one credential read (`APPROVED_DEFINER_EXECUTE`) without updating it, because I had not run that file. Updated deliberately, with a row in §8|
| | `test:e2e` exit 1: 19 passed, 1 failed (`lifecycle.spec.ts`, "a stack that fails to start leaves nothing behind")| Mine: the test lists the databases of the server before and after, and found one it did not make: the one my own `seed-initialization` run created while the step ran. Not a defect; I ran nothing beside a gate after that|
| Repeat | `format:check` exit 0; `lint` exit 1: seven parse errors in `e2e/test-results/evidence-probe-*`| Mine: the browser proof left its probe folders (TypeScript) where `eslint .` reads. It now removes its folder when its test passes and keeps it when it fails|
| | `pnpm test` exit 1: the Ops Portal started all 23 files; two API tests failed (`error-codes.test.ts`, `deployment-table.test.ts`)| Mine, and the worst: I ran `prettier --write` on this plan **from `apps/api`, where the repository's `.prettierignore` (relative to the root) did not apply**, and it reformatted every table (about 1400 lines). The two tests read the plan and found no row. The plan was restored from the earlier source package, every edit since was reapplied (its diff is 151 lines added and 28 removed, as before), and the lesson is kept: this plan, the specifications and the contracts are never formatted by a tool|
| | `test:db` exit 0 (44 files, 1236 passed, 2 skipped; `execution-budget.db.test.ts` 19 passed); `test:e2e` exit 0 (20 passed: the earlier 16, three browser proofs of the evidence policy and the canary)| On the final tree|
| Last| `format:check`, `lint`, `typecheck` exit 0; `pnpm test` exit 0 (five of five tasks: root 1191 tests with 1189 passed and 2 skipped, API 162 files with 4935 passed and 8 skipped, Ops Portal 23 files with 220, api-client 9 files with 250, harness 14 files with 305 passed and 2 skipped)| On the final tree, with the plan restored|

**The Ops Portal worker-start timeout, sampled for the first time in the run that failed** (the read-only sampler of §7.16, every 2 seconds). In the first minute of the Ops suite, the failing run and the two that started every file looked alike: CPU at 100% in 15 of 21 samples (mean 96%), 43 `node` processes and 4.2 GB, free memory no lower than 917 MB, against 19 of 22 (mean 99%), 39 processes, 3.6 GB and 760 MB in one passing run, and 19 of 21 (mean 96%), 45 processes, 3.5 GB and **297 MB** in the other. **The earlier suggestion that a fall of free memory separates the failing run is not supported:** the passing run had the least free memory, and the failing run's own lowest figure (468 MB) came before the Ops suite began. CPU saturation and process count do not separate them either. The cause is **not established**; it remains consistent with contention among the four suites turbo runs at once and is not shown to be it. The proposal of the audit and of §7.15 (a lower concurrency for the local `test` command, CI untouched) stands as the owner's decision.

**Not run, and why.** `test:browser` and `pnpm audit` (no browser behaviour and no dependency changed: the lockfile and every dependency list are as at `0cb2d6d`); every Linux, WSL2 and CI check, including the Linux run of this tree (the generator's POSIX-mode test and the Linux-only tests stay skipped on Windows); every proof of S0.23 that needs a test CA, including `openssl verify` of what the CA refuses; the two-build proof of the fixed policy (S0.63); `melarc_dev` was not touched.

**Review of the corrections (12 October 2026).** Two reviews of the corrections above judged them substantially sound, did not ask for the batch to be redone, and kept Gate 3 open. They found one defect in the code (C01), one in a contract sentence (C02) and two statements that said more than was proved. Each was checked against the code before it was changed; the changes are small and recorded here, with no separate handoff.

| Item| Verdict| What changed| Evidence|
|---|---|---|---|
| C01 Medium: the trigger of F02 refused a replacement that carried the very instant of the credential it replaced| **Confirmed in source.** The first form required the generation and the moment to move together, so a replacement stamped with an instant equal to the previous one (a transaction's `now()` is constant, so two replacements in one transaction do, and so does a clock that repeats) was refused, and a writer was pushed to invent a later instant, a time that is not true| The trigger holds the generation alone: it rises by exactly one and never moves back. `credential_established_at` changes only with a new generation, never alone, and **may equal the previous one**; a rehash changes neither. Migration `0016` is edited in place, because nothing is committed and `melarc_dev` was not migrated by this work (its builder script is changed with it; an owner who already migrated `melarc_dev` from the earlier tree must reset that database or write a new migration). OB-128, SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.9 and the schema comments say the same| `session-schema.db.test.ts`: a replacement carrying the same instant is allowed (new); refused: the moment changed alone, the moment moved back alone, a replacement with no generation, a jump of two, a generation that moves back, a credential cleared. `session-issue.db.test.ts`: a credential replaced at the **exact** same instant (no time advanced) is told from the one that was verified and the session is issued on the new generation (new). **RED and GREEN against PostgreSQL 18.6, 12 October 2026:** with the old trigger put back in the migration (restored by hash afterwards), exactly the two new positive tests fail (`session-schema.db.test.ts` "lets a replacement carry the very instant of the credential it replaced", `session-issue.db.test.ts` "tells a credential replaced at the very same instant ...") with the old trigger's own message ("a credential and its moment are replaced together"), and 91 of 93 pass; with the new trigger `session-schema`, `session-issue` and `session-signin` pass (3 files, 109 tests)|
| C02 Medium: the comment of `SignInStep` gave an order the code does not have| **Confirmed.** The comment said the steps run after the earlier session has been ended; the code runs them before, which is right: a credential, factor, challenge, grant or recovery row sits at the identity-lock level, and a session row is below it (§13.10a). The order is **not** changed| The comment, the DEPLOYMENT paragraph, `apps/api/CLAUDE.md` and OB-129 now say: steps run after the refusals are decided and before the earlier session is ended and the new one inserted; they are for identity proofs only; "database work" is a convention and a review boundary, not a runtime sandbox, so each first consumer is inspected and tested for rollback and concurrency (the first-consumer inspection, and the rollback and concurrency tests, stay). The Vendor and Rider tests use fixture directories locked by an advisory lock: platform evidence only, not proof of a real row lock| Comments and documents; the existing `session-signin.db.test.ts` is unchanged|
| Scope of "no one can read the hash"| The claim was about the API runtime login, not about the owner role or the migration login (which read what they own)| Reworded in the migration header, the schema comment, SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.9, DEPLOYMENT, F04 and finding 14 of §7.16: it is a boundary between application roles| Wording only|

### 7.18 The Gate 3 completion batch (12 October 2026)

**Authorization and scope.** The owner's instruction of 12 October 2026 authorizes, as one bounded batch with internal checkpoints: finishing S0.23 (trusted HTTPS harness) as far as the approved environment allows, S0.25 (CSRF, Origin and Fetch Metadata), S0.26 (authorization), S0.27 (the bundle-change primitive and its races; **S0.27 is the exit task of Gate 3**), and the step-by-step owner instructions for S0.28 (independent manual evidence, needed by S0.43, **not** a substitute for S0.27). No task is renumbered and no gate membership changes. S0.29 and every Stage 4 task are not started; no real sign-in, recovery or administration operation is added, so **fixture/platform evidence stays distinct from real-operation acceptance**. The new work is left unstaged, uncommitted and unpushed; no merge, deployment, dependency or toolchain change; `melarc_dev` and every `.env` are untouched; no CA or private key is generated on the owner's Windows machine and no trust store is changed.

**Baseline.** One scoped checkpoint commit of the reviewed closeout of §7.16 and §7.17 was authorized and made before any new code: `138d02ccada3399793ea914863c490afb74ce469` (parent `0cb2d6d21cbc3a4f3846ee5c0f4a587bde94f02a`, branch `feat/slice-000-gate-3`, 132 paths staged by explicit list: 51 modified and 81 new; unsigned, because no signing is configured). Before it, the working tree matched `melarc-s0.20-s0.24-review-corrected-source.zip` (SHA-256 `1d417900752eee14661e45b3c9a1dd10803805a1b323fa34563227fcff1f4278`) **file for file** (825 files; the manifest compared by hash), and no archive, `.env`, key, `tmp/`, Graphify output or local tool setting was in the commit. Nothing is pushed. While restoring it, one defect of an earlier edit of this plan was found and repaired: the heading of section 8 (the pinned tests) had been dropped from the plan by that edit.

**The owner's decisions of 12 October 2026, and how each is applied.**

| | Decision| Applied as| Where it is normative / proved|
|---|---|---|---|
| A| **Idle activity.** A failed CSRF check, a denied authorization and a rate-limit refusal do not extend the browser idle lifetime; unattended background polling does not keep an otherwise idle session alive. Not "every 4xx is inactivity": an authenticated, authorized action that receives validation or state-conflict feedback is still activity. Qualifying activity is defined **in server-side route policy**, never by a client header; absolute expiry is unchanged; the activity write is integrated with fresh session-state checks so that it cannot revive a terminated or expired session| Every route that needs a principal declares its activity policy (`qualifying` or `background`); a route with none stops the API at start. The authentication step **no longer writes activity**. A request records activity only **after** rate limit, authentication, CSRF and authorization, and only if the final outcome is not an authentication, CSRF, authorization or rate-limit refusal, by the session's row lock and then one conditional statement on the database's own clock (`terminated_at is null`, `now < expires_at`, not idle) whose affected-row count is read: a session ended, gone idle or expired during a lock wait is not revived, whether the other transaction changed the row or only locked it ([§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition), V01)| SECURITY_DESIGN.md §13.3; plan S0.24 (activity coalescing), OB-131; proved on a fixture route in `activity.db.test.ts` and the pipeline tests. **Obligation for future routes:** every polling or refresh route declares `background`; the first such route is a Stage 5 task's, with a test that it does not extend idle|
| B| **Missing or invalid required settings.** The runtime `SETTING_MISSING` and `SETTING_INVALID` failures map to the existing fixed, code-less `503`; no invented public `Error.code`, no setting value in a response or a log, no guessed `Retry-After`. The unknown-commit answer stays the separate code-less `500` with its fixed text and no `Retry-After`; an uncertain mutation is never retried automatically| The two error classes of the settings reader are operational failures of the platform filter| `operational-failure.ts`; `errors-and-enums.md` §4 and `ERROR_STATUS` (the two codes stay catalogue codes of the hub-settings paths of later slices, and the runtime failure does not use them on the wire)|
| C| **Authority floor.** A genuinely invalid, below-floor session is `401 SESSION_INVALID` **in authentication, before CSRF**; the stated refusal order is aligned. Kept distinct: an instance below `minimum_revision`, and an instance older than the session's `policy_revision`: both are the operational `503`, end no session and keep the readiness behaviour (`policy_superseded`). Only an instance qualified to evaluate current policy applies the invalid-session termination rule. Planned policy locks, authoritative revalidation and the mutation transaction boundary are preserved: the early check replaces neither the locked check nor the lock order| The authentication step compares the session with the bundle's `reduction_floor` and, for a Vendor or a Rider, with the fixed policy's `minimum_revision` and this build's compiled revision, without a lock (a read is ordered before a reduction it races). A state-changing operation repeats the comparison **under the shared policy locks** in its own transaction (`policy, identity, session`), which is the authoritative one| permission-enforcement.md §5.1 step 2; SECURITY_DESIGN.md §13.10 and §13.10a; OB-130 closed here; proved with fixture sessions on real PostgreSQL. The two-real-build proof stays S0.63's|
| D| **Local test scheduling.** An explicit `test:local` script that runs the same root and package tests with Turbo package concurrency limited to one; canonical CI execution unchanged; every test and assertion kept, fail-on-first-error, zero automatic retries, no timeout raised. Described as bounded local scheduling and not as a root cause of the Ops Portal worker-start timeout| Root `package.json` and `DEVELOPMENT.md`; the script inventory guard updated where the command requires it| `DEVELOPMENT.md`; `scripts/` guards|
| E| **Credential boundary.** The column privilege and the narrow `SECURITY DEFINER` function stay; no credential-table split; search path, schema qualification, restricted `EXECUTE`, owner and posture checks and the denial to other contexts are preserved; no runtime grant is widened to make a test pass| Nothing changes| §7.17, F04|
| F| **Pending matters.** The status disagreements of later slices stay with their owning tasks. **OB-100 is not approval to invent an in-progress code**: its retained requirements are inspected and the concurrent behaviour reproduced on the fixture before its consumer; if no approved status or envelope resolves it, the exact narrow proposal and the supporting scenario are prepared and the dependent acceptance item stays **blocked** while independent work finishes| The fixture reproduces two requests under one key with the first held open; the pipeline's answer to `IN_PROGRESS` is the fail-closed operational answer **only until the owner names the code**| OB-100 (below)|

**Task state in this batch** (nothing here is a claim that Gate 3 has passed).

| Task| State| Evidence reached| Blocked or owed|
|---|---|---|---|
| S0.25| Built| *Fixture/platform verified* on Windows over real HTTP and a real PostgreSQL: the Origin, Fetch Metadata and synchronizer-token decisions, exact-origin CORS, the Rider's bearer path excluded, `auth.csrf.rejected` (`GBT-W1`, `W2`, `W4`, `W5`, `W6`, `W7`, `W10`, `W11`, `W12`, `W15`)| The browser specs of what each engine really sends are built and unrun (S0.23's first run); the Rider's native path is proved with fixture sessions only (S0.59) |
| S0.26| Built| *Fixture/platform verified*: the surface gate, the permission from the snapshot, the tier, the scope of the authorizing grant, the existence rule and the scope probe, the authority floors in authentication (decision C), activity by route policy and outcome (decision A), the revalidating mutation runner, `policy_superseded`, and the refusal order on a fixture operation, the tier at step 6 added to it by the review of 12 October 2026 ([§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition)) (`AC-SLICE-000-26`, `-29`, `-30`, `-44`, `-114`, `-130`; Families B and GS; `OB-131`)| The two-build proof of the fixed policy (S0.63); real routes and record entities (S0.31 onward); the answer to OB-100 |
| S0.27| Built; **the exit task of Gate 3**| *Fixture/platform verified* on a real PostgreSQL: the privilege matrix, a reduction against a sign-in and against a state-changing request in both orders, additions that retain snapshots, two writers, deadlines, no leaked lock, strict isolation and the planned set (§13.10a 1 to 7, 9 and 12; `GBT-B12`, `GBT-B13`; `AC-SLICE-000-05`)| Randomised interleavings and starvation (§13.10a 8 and 10; S0.64); two builds (13; S0.63); SLICE-009's edit operation and how it reaches the functions |
| S0.23| Built as far as the approved environment allows (§7.16 and below); **the CA, trust and browser proofs have not run**| The harness's own tests; the plain `test:e2e` (Chromium over HTTP) unchanged in result| The first run of `pnpm run test:e2e:https` in CI, or on a machine cleared for key material |
