**What was built.** Each task was built test first and then held to deliberate breaks of its own code (the "Break caught" comments in the tests; the counts are below). The state cells of the obligations this batch ran or changed (OB-01 to OB-07, OB-09, OB-12, OB-20, OB-21, OB-28, OB-63, OB-65, OB-72, OB-73, OB-75, OB-100, OB-130, OB-131) are updated where they stand and are not restated here.

- **S0.25, CSRF, Origin and Fetch Metadata** (`platform/auth/csrf/`, `platform/routes/csrf.guard.ts`, `platform/http/cors.ts`; step 3 of `AC-SLICE-000-130`). One pure decision in a fixed order, the first failure the one the audit names and every failure outwardly the same `403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED`: the `Origin` must equal, as the string a browser writes, an origin configured for the surface (a missing, repeated, `null`, malformed or unlisted value is refused; the header is read from the raw lines, because Node joins repeated ones); Fetch Metadata is read where the browser sends it (`Sec-Fetch-Site` `same-origin` or absent; anything else, a repeated line included, is refused); and for a cookie-authenticated request the synchronizer token is compared in constant time with the hash the session stored (a missing, repeated or wrong header, and a session that holds no hash, are mismatches). The guard decides three cases by what the request is authenticated with and never by anything the client says: a browser pre-session mutation (an `anonymous` route, judged against the origins of the surfaces it serves), a cookie session (judged against its own surface's origin: a staff session is the Ops origin's, a vendor session the Vendor origin's) and a native operation (an `anonymous` route declared `native`, or a Rider bearer session: no Origin, token or Fetch Metadata is required). Safe methods pass; a route that needs a principal and holds no session is refused (this guard must never be the one that lets such a request on); a route that states no client is held to the browser rule on both surfaces. `auth.csrf.rejected` carries the route as matched and the check that failed, never the token, its hash or the `Origin` as sent, and a failure to record it never decides the answer. Credentialed CORS answers a configured origin with the configured string (never the request's own, never `*`), gives an unlisted origin no header and refuses its preflight (`403`, no error code), answers a preflight itself before routing with a fixed list of methods and headers, and sends `Vary: Origin`. Tests: `csrf-decision.test.ts`, `cors.test.ts`, `csrf.guard.test.ts` and, over real HTTP with real sessions, `csrf-guard.db.test.ts`.
- **S0.26, authorization** (`platform/authorization/`, `platform/routes/authorization.guard.ts`, `platform/auth/sessions/`). *The guard* (steps 4 to 7): the surface gate (the route names the surfaces it serves; a Vendor session on an Ops-only operation fails before ownership is considered), the permission from the **snapshot** the session was issued with (never the live bundle, a role or a bundle's name), the scope of **the grant that authorizes this operation** intersected with what the session reaches (`effective-scope.ts`: `own` is the session's hubs, `all` the explicit all-hub mode, `none` and a `self` operation an empty set, and an empty set means no rows, never all rows), the tier (`tier-policy.ts`: `A < S < P`, a grant with no tier meets no requirement, `INSUFFICIENT_AUTHORITY` is a held key at too low a tier and `PERMISSION_DENIED` a key not held, never both; the route declares it, `tier: { minimum, rule }`, and the guard decides it at step 6, before the scope and the validation: [§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition), F01) and the existence rule for a record named in the path (`record-scope.ts`: `404 NOT_FOUND`, or for staff on a hub-scoped entity `403 HUB_SCOPE_VIOLATION` by the scope probe; a Vendor and a Rider are answered `NOT_FOUND` by the same code path as a record that does not exist; `RECORD_ENTITIES` is empty until a slice adds a table that an operation addresses by id). The decision is kept beside the request (`AuthorizedRequest`). Route declarations now state their surfaces, an activity policy, optionally a record and optionally a tier, and a route that needs a principal and states no surfaces or no activity policy stops the API at start. *The runner:* `OperationRunner.read` takes no policy lock and refuses a state-changing request; `OperationRunner.mutate` runs the S0.7 toolkit (the shared lock on the acting session's bundle and, for a Vendor or a Rider, on the fixed policy; the session read again under them and judged on one fresh database instant), so a session that a reduction ended while the request waited is `401` and nothing the use case would have written is written (`OB-131`). *The authority floors* (decision C, `session-authority.ts`): authentication compares a session with its bundle's `reduction_floor` and, for a Vendor or a Rider, with the fixed policy's `minimum_revision` and this build's compiled revision, without a lock; a genuinely below-floor session is `401 SESSION_INVALID` before CSRF is considered and is ended `AUTHORITY_CHANGED` with one enhanced `auth.session.authority_revoked`; an instance that cannot be sure of the policy (no compiled revision, none seeded, below the floor, older than the session) answers the operational `503`, ends nothing, and `/readyz` reports `policy_superseded` (`policy-readiness.ts`). *Activity* (decision A): authentication no longer writes it; `SessionActivityInterceptor` records it after the guards by the route's declared policy and the outcome, through `ActivityRecorder`: the session's row lock first, then one conditional statement on the database's clock that finds the session live (a refusal of authority, a failure of the platform and a `background` route never extend it; a validation or state answer to an authorized action does; the absolute expiry never moves). *Evidence:* `authorization-core.test.ts`, `operation-runner.test.ts`, `session-authority.test.ts` and `session-activity.interceptor.test.ts` (units) and `authorization.db.test.ts`, `authority.db.test.ts`, `activity.db.test.ts` and `refusal-order.db.test.ts` (a fixture operation of the test, in no route inventory and no `IMPLEMENTED_OPERATIONS`, proves the whole order, that a refused request has no side effect and that a command's idempotency record, effect and answer are one unit of work).
- **S0.27, the bundle-change primitive and its races** (migration `0017`, `platform/authorization/policy-change/`, `seed-helper.ts`, `migration-lint.ts`). **Decided and recorded: SQL functions, not application code behind a command**, because the registry names the migration login as the identity for a policy change after the seed, a migration is SQL, and the only way into a forced-row-level-security table that this codebase accepts is a `SECURITY DEFINER` function with a fixed search path and a restricted `EXECUTE` (migration `0005` is the precedent). `melarc.change_role_bundle` and `melarc.change_fixed_policy` take the exclusive lock in the canonical order, read the current revision, write the content and the revision and, on a **reduction**, raise `reduction_floor` (or `minimum_revision`) and end every `ACTIVE` session recorded below it with `AUTHORITY_CHANGED` and one enhanced `auth.session.authority_revoked` each, **in the same transaction** (the events are the ones `endForAuthority` writes, and a test compares them); an addition raises the revision and ends nothing; a change of the name alone raises nothing. A reduction is the removal of a grant or a narrowing of its hub scope (`none < own < all`), its ownership scope (`own` and `assigned` are not ordered: a move between them is a reduction) or its tier (`none < A < S < P`, where a null tier is *not applicable* and ranks below every tier: OB-75); a change that cannot be shown to be a widening is a reduction, because that can only end more sessions, never fewer. The functions run at `READ COMMITTED` only, refuse the protocol's violations (an upgrade, a bundle lock after a fixed lock, a lower key after a higher one), accept a correlation id of a fixed grammar and run only as the migration login; the runtime, the worker and the provisioning login have no `EXECUTE` on them and no table privilege is given to anyone. `melarc.lock_policy_keys` takes a seed's or a release step's exclusive keys in one call in canonical order, and the seed now takes its keys with it. The migration lint refuses a direct write of a policy table unless the migration says why (`-- allow-policy-dml:`). A new task capability, `policy_change`, is asserted only by these functions and is in the registry. `changeRoleBundle` and `changeFixedPolicy` (TypeScript) are the door for a caller that changes one object, and `PolicyChangeError` names the database's refusals. *Evidence* (`policy-change.db.test.ts`, `policy-change-races.db.test.ts`, on a real PostgreSQL 18.6): the privilege matrix over every technical login; a reduction against a sign-in and against a state-changing request in both orders (the session of a sign-in that was ahead is ended; one that starts after reads the reduced content; a request already past its lock finishes whole before the reduction proceeds; one that waits meets an ended session and does nothing); additions that retain snapshots, followed by a reduction that ends every session below its floor; two writers that serialise; a writer that does not hold up a distinct key; an exclusive lock that cannot be taken in time leaves nothing; no advisory lock on the connection afterwards; a stricter isolation level refused; the classification matrices; the SQL lock keys equal to the toolkit's, as the server reports them in `pg_locks`; and two steps that take a bundle and then the Vendor policy never deadlock.
- **S0.23, the trusted HTTPS harness** (`e2e/harness/`, `e2e/tests/`, `e2e/playwright.config.ts`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; added to the partial build of §7.16). *Built, with no certificate ever made on this machine:* `https-front.ts`, a TLS-terminating front in front of the Ops origin that stands in for the production edge (the shell's tested content security policy with `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `nosniff`, `no-referrer`, HSTS; `Set-Cookie` forwarded unchanged; `GBT-W9`); `engine-trust.ts`, a store of its own for each engine in the run's directory (Chromium: the NSS database of a home directory made for the run, `HOME` set for the browser process only; Firefox: a profile seeded with the CA, kept as a template and copied for each test, from which a persistent context is launched; WebKit: `SSL_CERT_FILE` for the browser process only, **a candidate that only a run can show to work**; the first hosted run showed it ignored, and WebKit's trust is now the system store of a GitHub-hosted runner: [§7.21](#721-the-first-hosted-run-of-the-checkpoint-12-october-2026-its-diagnosis-and-the-correction)), with `certutil` behind a parameter so that no test runs a tool; `test-ca.ts`, which now proves with `openssl verify`, before the CA's key is deleted, that the leaf verifies for `localhost` and `127.0.0.1` and that a second leaf for `example.com`, signed by the same CA and key, does **not** (a permitted-subtree refusal; any other refusal does not count), and removes a CA that does not refuse the outside name; `https-run.ts` and `pnpm run test:e2e:https`, one command that refuses on Windows and macOS and on Linux outside CI unless the person has declared the machine cleared for key material (`MELARC_E2E_KEYS_CLEARED=1`), makes the CA in a private directory outside the checkout, runs the HTTPS projects in two runs (an ordinary and a secret one for Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, and WebKit's refusal in a project of its own: [§7.21](#721-the-first-hosted-run-of-the-checkpoint-12-october-2026-its-diagnosis-and-the-correction)) and removes the directory in a `finally`; branded Chrome and Edge projects, which no command runs; `https-test.ts` and `https/secret-test.ts` (without test certificates a spec is skipped with its reason on a developer machine and **fails in CI**); and five specs: `https-trust.spec.ts` (the per-engine spike; its negative control must be that engine's own certificate diagnostic, beside a probe that the endpoint is up and validates against the CA: [§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition), F03), `https-isolation.spec.ts` (one test's cookie and local storage are not there in the next, and the CA is still trusted: F04), `https-headers.spec.ts` (`GBT-W8`, `W9`), `https-cookies.secret.test.ts` (`GBT-W13`: each engine stores the product's cookies as set, script reads the CSRF token and not the session cookie, both are sent back, rotated and expired) and `https-origin.secret.test.ts` (what each engine really sends that S0.25 judges). The technical routes `cookies/issue`, `cookies/expire` and `echo` exist only in the test entry point (they set or echo cookie and header **names**, never values), are in no inventory and are fixture evidence only. `pnpm run test:e2e` now runs the plain projects (Chromium over HTTP) only. In CI the `integrated` job installs `libnss3-tools` and runs `pnpm run test:e2e:https` as a mandatory step (`ci.yml`, `REQUIRED_COMMANDS`, the aggregate and `ci-workflow.test.ts` together). *Not run, and reported blocked:* the generator against a real `openssl`, the stores against a real `certutil`, every engine's trust of the CA, the HTTPS origin in a browser, WebKit's mechanism and the branded smoke. The restriction on making a CA or a key and on changing trust on the owner's Windows machine was kept; the existence of OpenSSL or of a test script was not read as permission.
- **S0.28, accessibility baseline evidence.** The runbook's evidence item 8 now carries the owner's steps (`BOOTSTRAP_B0_RUNBOOK.md`, "How to produce it"): start the shell, record the environment, keyboard-only passes, NVDA with Firefox and with Chrome or Edge, Windows contrast themes, motion, 200 % text and narrow widths, the browsers as products with Safari recorded as not covered, and the signed note. No agent can perform, sign or claim it. It certifies nothing about S0.20 to S0.27, is no substitute for S0.27, and has one dependant, S0.43.

**Documents changed with the code**, each in the change that needed it: SECURITY_DESIGN.md (§13.10a, "The primitive"; the obligations run), MIGRATION_AND_SEEDING.md, DEPLOYMENT_AND_ENVIRONMENTS.md §12.5 ("What S0.25, S0.26 and S0.27 applied"), data-scope-registry.md (`policy_change`; the writers of the policy tables), permission-enforcement.md, errors-and-enums.md (the code-less `503` for a setting), BOOTSTRAP_B0_RUNBOOK.md (the S0.28 steps), DEVELOPMENT.md (`test:local`, `test:e2e:https`), e2e/README.md, e2e/CLAUDE.md and apps/api/CLAUDE.md.

**Break checks.** Deliberate breaks of the batch's new code, each restored by hash; no broad campaign. S0.25: 36, run again on the final tree: all caught but one equivalent (the request's own origin is the configured string whenever the reflecting line is reached); three that the database test alone does not catch are caught by the guard's unit test, and one that did not apply the first time (a formatting change had moved the text) was written again and caught. S0.26: 25 in the first set; seven survivors showed gaps (the below-floor comparison, the older-instance comparison, the idempotent ending, the state-changing flag, the rule that a platform failure is not activity, the absolute-end clause of the activity statement and the recorder's failure), tests were added, the new guard that keeps a state-changing request off the lock-free path was itself broken, and the eight affected breaks were run again on the final tree: all caught; one other mutant is equivalent and kept as defence in depth. S0.27: 21 (the classification, the lock order, the audit event, the isolation guard, the privilege, the correlation grammar and the seed's use of the lock function): all caught.

**Findings and decisions for the Product Owner** (nothing here was defaulted or invented):

1. **The primitive is two `SECURITY DEFINER` functions under a new task capability, `policy_change`.** Forced row-level security leaves nobody an `UPDATE` or a `DELETE` on the three policy tables (the owner and the migration login included), and the runtime may hold `SELECT` only, so the registry's "writers: seed, migration" had no way to act once the seed had run. The functions are owned by `melarc_owner` and executable by the migration login alone; the capability can be asserted as a label by the runtime logins and reaches nothing for them, because they hold `SELECT` on these tables and no more (`policy-change.db.test.ts` proves it for every login). This is a new security surface; the registry and SECURITY_DESIGN.md §13.10a are updated in the same change. **Approved by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026)).** The alternative, application code behind a provisioning command, would need the same functions for the writes and privileges to end sessions and write the audit that only the runtime holds.
2. **How SLICE-009's edit operation reaches the functions is undecided, and no privilege was widened.** The runtime has no `EXECUTE` on them and no table privilege (the registry's `api` writer for the policy tables was withdrawn by the decision on OB-73). The slice chooses: one `EXECUTE` grant to the runtime on one function (a reviewed line in `APPROVED_DEFINER_EXECUTE` and in the matrix of `policy-change.db.test.ts`), or a privileged job run as the migration login. This is the conflict, presented and not resolved.
3. **A move between `own` and `assigned` ownership is treated as a reduction**, as is any change whose direction the orders cannot show (`any` ranks above both; the two are not ordered). A wrong guess costs sessions ended that need not have been, never sessions kept. **Approved by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026)).**
4. **The primitive changes a bundle's name and the complete set of its grants, and nothing else.** It does not change a bundle's audience or its `privileged` flag; a change to either alters who may approve whom, and no document of this slice says how it is made. The slice that needs it decides.
5. **The seed is moved onto the primitive's locking, not onto its diff.** `runSeed` takes every key it creates with `melarc.lock_policy_keys` (one SQL ordering for every writer) and inserts revision 1 and floor 1 into an empty set through `melarc.initialize_reference_data`; an insert is not a change, so it calls neither change function.
6. **The correlation id of a change is a grammar** (`^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:/#-]{0,127}$`) that checks shape and nothing more: it is validation and no screen for a secret or for personal data, so a caller passes an identifier it generated or the fixed label of a release step and never text that a user supplied (the wording was corrected on the review of 12 October 2026, [§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition)). **Approved by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026)).**
7. **Authentication now reads the authority on every authenticated request**: one read of `role_bundle` and, for a Vendor or a Rider, one of `fixed_permission_policy`, inside the authentication transaction that already exists (no extra connection and no lock). It is an engineering cost that S0.65 measures; development-qualified, not production-qualified.
8. **CI gains a package step and a longer budget.** The `integrated` job installs `libnss3-tools` (`sudo apt-get install --no-install-recommends --yes`) and runs `pnpm run test:e2e:https` after `pnpm run test:e2e`; its `timeout-minutes` goes from 25 to 30 for the added suite. The package is a tool of the runner and not a dependency of the repository, and no trust store of the runner is changed *(superseded for WebKit alone: [§7.21](#721-the-first-hosted-run-of-the-checkpoint-12-october-2026-its-diagnosis-and-the-correction); the owner's decision of 12 October 2026 puts the disposable CA in the system store of the GitHub-hosted runner for WebKit's proofs)*. **Approved by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026)): the package and the 30-minute budget.** The first hosted run exercises a workflow change this large: if a step is wrong, that run shows it.
9. **`pnpm run test:e2e` runs Chromium over plain HTTP only**; Firefox and WebKit run in `test:e2e:https`, which exists only on Linux (CI, or a machine cleared for key material). On the developer's Windows workstation there is no automated HTTPS run, because Chromium, Chrome and Edge use the operating system's trust store there and a persistent machine-wide trust change needs the owner's exact installation and removal plan first ([§10](#10-owner-resolutions--the-status-of-every-item), row 4). `test:browser` (the shell and components in three engines over plain HTTP) is unchanged.
10. **WebKit's trust mechanism is a candidate** (`SSL_CERT_FILE` for the browser process): whether Playwright's WebKit build honours it is what the first run shows; until it does, WebKit is reported blocked and not green. *(Resolved by the first hosted run: it is ignored, and WebKit's trust is the system store of a GitHub-hosted runner by the owner's decision of 12 October 2026: [§7.21](#721-the-first-hosted-run-of-the-checkpoint-12-october-2026-its-diagnosis-and-the-correction).)* The branded Chrome and Edge smoke is a person's.
11. **`melarc_dev` holds only migration `0000`** (a strictly read-only comparison of its journal table with the folder, 12 October 2026; nothing was changed). No earlier `0016` was ever applied there, so there is no applied history to correct and no forward migration to write; `db:migrate`, when the owner runs it, applies `0001` to `0017` in one run. The folder and the journal agree.
12. **Disposable databases were left on the local server** by runs that timed out (nine marked `melarc_test_*` databases when the gate began; `pnpm --filter @melarc/api run db:orphans` lists them and changes nothing). `db:orphans --drop` was not authorized in this batch; when five of them made one database test fail (the gate record below) the owner authorized dropping exactly those five on 12 October 2026, and only those were dropped. Four remain (`melarc_test_0ad977c7`, `_36dad696`, `_53fec8b3`, `_7500ca4d`; none holds a privilege of the capability role) and one with no marker, `melarc_test_unmarked12c5bfb2`, which `db:orphans` does not list and nothing here touched. They are the owner's to remove. Some are from this batch's timed-out runs; the rest cannot be attributed. *(Superseded: on 12 October 2026 the owner authorized the removal of the ten marked ones, which were removed, and the three unmarked ones were inspected and kept: [§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026).)*
13. **OB-100: the exact narrow proposal** (decision F). *What the retained requirements say* (SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.16): a command's `begin`, effect and `complete` are one transaction of the caller; the record is visible to anyone else only at commit, and then already `COMPLETED`; a concurrent duplicate does not see `IN_PROGRESS` at all but waits on the unique index and then replays; a committed `IN_PROGRESS` record exists only if a command was assembled wrongly. *What the fixture shows* (`refusal-order.db.test.ts`): a command that fails after `begin` rolls the record back with its effect and the retry owns the command; a failure in the middle of the write rolls the record and the effect back together; two concurrent requests under one key, the first held open by a row lock and both watched on the server (never timed), leave the second waiting on the unique index until the first commits, then replaying its answer, with the record changed once; a record planted `IN_PROGRESS` (what a defect would leave) is not answered as a success or a replay, and the pipeline answers the fixed code-less `500` of the unexpected failure with nothing changed. **So no client-visible outcome exists for a correct command, and the missing code would be for a defect.** *Proposal:* (1) add no contract error code: the `IN_PROGRESS` outcome of the store is an internal invariant failure, answered by the existing fixed code-less `500` with the request id, logged with the operation and the principal's type (never the key) and counted, so that S0.68 can alert on it; (2) who reclaims such a record is the open part of OB-113 and OB-114 (no identity holds `DELETE` on the table), and the narrowest reading is that the existing retention cleanup ends it after the replay window, which needs the owner's identity and period; (3) if the owner prefers a client-visible answer instead, `STATE_CONFLICT` is the nearest code and would mislead a client that can safely retry, so the contract would need a new code. **Until the owner decides, the dependent acceptance item stays blocked:** the fixture keeps its `it.todo` for the HTTP answer and the code, and no criterion is reported passed on this path. *(Refined by the review of 12 October 2026, [§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition): the code-less `500` is for a committed, stranded `IN_PROGRESS` record only, which a defect of whoever assembled the command leaves; a duplicate whose wait runs out of time is the platform's operational failure, and that distinction is tested.)* *(Approved by the owner on 12 October 2026, [§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026): the code-less `500` is the answer for a committed, stranded `IN_PROGRESS` record only, and there is no automatic reclamation, so the retention-cleanup reading in point (2) is not adopted.)*
14. **Later slices' status disagreements** (41 operations outside the thirty-eight of this slice, §7.16 finding 3) are untouched, as decision F says; each slice that builds one resolves its own.

**Gate of the batch** (uncached, `TURBO_FORCE=true`, Node 24.21.0, pnpm 11.1.3, Windows 11, a local PostgreSQL 18.6; run once on the tree handed over, from 13:47 to 14:05 UTC: `138d02c` plus 128 changed paths, 79 modified and 49 new, unstaged and unchanged from the start of the gate to its end). Every command exited 0 except one, reported below: `format:check` (18 s), `lint` (82 s), `typecheck` (four packages, 22 s), `build` (two tasks, none cached), `contract:check` (0 operations compared: the machinery only), `api-client:check` and `db:check`; `test:local` (`pnpm run test:root && turbo run test --concurrency=1`, decision D; 280 s, five of five tasks, none cached): root 1205 tests (1203 passed, 2 skipped), API 170 files (5196 passed, 8 skipped), Ops Portal 23 files (220 passed), api-client 9 files (250 passed), harness 17 files (366 passed, 2 skipped); `test:browser` over Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`MELARC_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit`, plain HTTP): 75 tests, 74 passed and 1 skipped (WebKit cannot emulate forced colours); `test:e2e`: 20 passed (Chromium over plain HTTP).

- **`test:db` exited 1 once: 1477 passed, 2 skipped, 1 todo and one failure.** The failure is `catalog-invariants.db.test.ts`, "a technical role that does not exist" (`role "melarc_worker_cap_credential_delivery" cannot be dropped because some objects depend on it`). *The reason, established before anything was rerun:* that case drops the cluster-wide capability role inside a transaction it rolls back, which PostgreSQL refuses while any other database on the server holds a privilege granted to the role. Five leftover `melarc_test_*` databases of earlier runs that timed out (marked, no session connected) held privileges granted to the role (23 objects each); a read-only query of `pg_shdepend` named them and no other dependant, and the same test had passed in the earlier gate of 12 October (`test:db` 1239 passed), before they were left. It is a fragility of that test's dependence on the rest of the server, not a defect of the code under test; the review that followed called it a harness defect, and the test was changed to rename the role inside the transaction it rolls back instead of dropping it ([§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition)). The owner authorized dropping exactly those five (`db:orphans --drop`, 12 October 2026); the file then passed alone (54 tests) and the whole of `test:db` was run again, uncached, on the unchanged tree: **exit 0, 51 files, 1478 passed, 2 skipped, 1 todo.** The files of S0.25 to S0.27 in it: `csrf-guard` 49 tests, `authority` 13, `authorization` 22, `activity` 18, `refusal-order` 15 (and the `it.todo` of OB-100), `policy-change` 107, `policy-change-races` 12; `execution-budget.db.test.ts` 19 passed in both runs.
- **The Ops Portal worker-start timeout did not recur:** all 23 Ops files started under `test:local` (package concurrency one). The canonical `pnpm test` at default concurrency, the form CI runs, was not run, so this is no diagnosis; OB-91 and [§7.15](#715-gate-2-audit-corrections-11-october-2026-disposition) stand. The read-only sampler (every 2 seconds over the whole gate, 335 samples) saw mean CPU 37% and 99% or more in 22 samples, free memory no lower than 545 MB, and at most 17 `node` processes (2.6 GB).
- **Found by a preflight run of `lint` and `typecheck` before the gate, and fixed first:** the root typecheck was broken by my own edit, because `tools/database/safety.ts` imported `platform/database/technical-roles.ts` and a root script imports `safety.ts` under Node's own type stripping (it is import-free again, and `safety.test.ts` holds the two spellings of the migration role equal; shown RED by a deliberate break); and 39 lint findings in tests and in the e2e harness that earlier work had not yet been linted for (untyped query rows, void-returning arrow bodies, an unused binding, a dynamic `delete`, an unbound method, an unnecessary assertion), fixed at their cause with no rule disabled and no assertion weakened. The gate's own `lint` and `typecheck` passed.
- **Skipped, not passed:** API 8 (the five real-`SIGTERM` process tests of Stage 1, the delivery worker's real-`SIGTERM` process test, and the two file-mode tests of the capture sink, which a Windows file system cannot answer), `test:db` 2 (`api-process.db.test.ts` and `delivery-worker-process.db.test.ts`, real `SIGTERM`), root 2, harness 2 (the generator's POSIX-mode test and one Linux-only test of the managed-process harness), browser 1; each needs Linux, WSL2 or CI.
- **Not run, and why:** `test:e2e:https` (Linux only; it refuses on this machine, where no CA may be made), so every proof of S0.23 that needs a CA, `certutil` or an engine's trust store, WebKit's mechanism and the branded Chrome and Edge smoke; the canonical `pnpm test`; `pnpm audit` (no dependency changed: the lockfile and every dependency list are as at the checkpoint); every Linux, WSL2 and CI check, including a hosted run of this tree (nothing is pushed). `melarc_dev` was not changed: a strictly read-only comparison of its journal table is the only thing done to it.
- **Tooling:** Graphify 0.9.85 was used for orientation only (`graphify update .` after the code changes; no semantic pass); its output is local, git-ignored and not evidence.

**Gate 3 status.** Not passed and not claimed. *Proved, as fixture/platform evidence on Windows:* S0.20 to S0.27 are built and their tests are green under `test:db` and `test:browser`, and S0.27 reaches each of them through its Needs. *What stands in the way:* (1) S0.23 is not complete: no CA, `certutil`, engine trust or HTTPS origin has run, and the first hosted run of `pnpm run test:e2e:https` (or a run on a machine the owner clears for key material) is that proof; (2) S0.28 is the owner's evidence (no substitute for S0.27, and needed by S0.43); (3) the owner's decisions in findings 1 to 3, 6, 8 and 13 above (approved on 12 October 2026 except finding 2: [§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026)); (4) a hosted Linux run of the final revision. Verdict of the first gate: local implementation verified, with pending evidence. **The independent review that followed returned FIX REQUIRED; its corrections, and the gate of the corrected tree, are in [§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition).**

### 7.19 Independent review of the Gate 3 completion batch (12 October 2026): disposition

**What this is.** An independent review of the source package of [§7.18](#718-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026) (`melarc-gate-3-completion-source.zip`, SHA-256 `c60b2cf7b723c21eb15fcd1b4b656b6e75496900faa130a2ba2f2c0c77415ade`, 874 files on `138d02c` with 128 changes) returned **FIX REQUIRED**: four source-level findings (F01 to F04), one verification question (V01), recommended answers to the owner's pending decisions and advice on the length of a batch. It ran no PostgreSQL and no browser (its checks were isolated runs of the scope resolver's source with stubbed dependencies and of the negative test's predicate), so each finding was checked here against the code and, where it could be, reproduced against PostgreSQL 18.6 before anything was changed. Each was fixed test first, and each new test was seen to fail for the stated reason. Nothing here is a claim that Gate 3 has passed, and nothing is committed, staged or pushed.

| Finding| Verdict| What changed| Evidence|
|---|---|---|---|
| F01 High: the tier was left to the operation, which runs after validation| **Confirmed, and reproduced**: a caller with the key and not the tier, sending a malformed body, got `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` where `403 INSUFFICIENT_AUTHORITY` is required; the fixture never called `requireTier`| A route declares its tier, `tier: { minimum?, rule? }`, and the authorization guard decides it at step 6. The **minimum** is decided before anything is looked up. A **rule** (`TIER_RULES`, empty until a slice adds one) derives what the target needs from facts of its own record and is asked once the caller can see the record, through the operation's own transaction and so under the caller's row-level security: no privileged read, no role-name comparison, never a value from the request. An unknown rule stops the API at start, and a rule that answers anything but a tier or `null` fails closed. A caller who cannot see the record is answered by step 7 (the ambiguity below). `OperationRunner.mutate` judges the requirement again, under the locks, before the work, and a use case that locks its target calls `OperationRunner.requireTargetTier` after the lock| `refusal-order.db.test.ts` over real HTTP: insufficient tier with a malformed body; below the minimum with a record outside the hubs, an absent one and a visible one, each `INSUFFICIENT_AUTHORITY`; an invisible record answered by the scope; a missing permission wins over the tier; a sufficient tier proceeds; a refusal leaves no record changed, no key spent and no event; a requirement that changed while the request waited (for the row, and for the policy lock) is refused and writes nothing. Units for the declaration, the rule helper, the runner and the inventory. Three deliberate breaks of the new code, all caught|
| F02 High for the reusable boundary: the scope probe equated existence with an out-of-hub record| **Confirmed, and reproduced** with a real RLS fixture (two RED tests)| A miss is `HUB_SCOPE_VIOLATION` only when the probed hub is outside the effective scope the operation runs under. An existing record in a hub the operation reaches that another restriction hides, and any miss under an all-hub grant, is `NOT_FOUND`; a Vendor and a Rider stay probe-free. SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.4b and permission-enforcement.md §5.1 say so| `authorization.db.test.ts`, with a table that hides rows by hub **and** by owner: visible, outside the hubs (mine and others'), absent, hidden by its owner in an authorized hub, an all-hub caller with another restriction's miss, and a Vendor with the same shape and no probe. One deliberate break, caught|
| F03 Medium: the HTTPS negative proof accepted unrelated navigation failures| **Confirmed**: the old predicate (not a refused connection) accepts a timeout, `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` and a closed page, context or browser| `harness/trust-failure.ts`: only the engine's own certificate diagnostic is evidence (Chromium `net::ERR_CERT_AUTHORITY_INVALID`; Firefox `SEC_ERROR_UNKNOWN_ISSUER` or `SSL_ERROR_UNKNOWN_CA_ALERT`; WebKit by platform: Linux `Unacceptable TLS certificate`, macOS `The certificate for this server is invalid`, Windows `SSL peer certificate or SSH remote key was not OK`), and a deadline, a name or connection failure, a blocked request, a crash or a closed target is never one, even beside a certificate word. A failure with any other signature fails the test to be diagnosed. `harness/https-probe.ts` shows, in the same test, that the endpoint answers a client that trusts the CA. The browser's version and the verdict are attached. The pinned builds on this machine were searched for the names (both Firefox names, the Windows WebKit message, Chromium's `CERT_AUTHORITY_INVALID`); the Linux confirmation is the first run's| `trust-failure.test.ts` (30 tests with the probe's): the review's examples and the other ways a navigation fails are rejected, each engine's diagnostic is accepted, another engine's is not. **Unrun in a browser**|
| F04 Medium: Firefox tests shared one persistent profile| **Confirmed**: every Firefox context launched from the same directory, and closing a persistent context does not delete its profile| The seeded profile is a **template that no browser is launched from** (`EngineTrust.profileTemplate`); the fixture gives each test a private copy beside it (`harness/fresh-profile.ts`), launches the persistent context from the copy, and runs the teardown whatever the test did (the launch failing included): it closes the context and removes the copy, swallows neither failure, and reports it. A test that passed fails with a `ProfileTeardownError` naming every step that failed; a test that failed keeps its own failure, the very same error, and the failure of the teardown is reported beside it (the follow-up of the second review, below). `tests/https-isolation.spec.ts` shows it on the real fixture in each engine: one test stores a persistent cookie and a local-storage entry, the next has neither and still validates the CA| `fresh-profile.test.ts`: the copy is whole, separate from the template, one per call, private, removed; the template is never what is launched from; what one test wrote is gone in the next; the copy goes when the test throws, the context will not close, or the launch fails; a failed teardown fails a test that passed, and beside a test that failed it is reported without replacing that failure. **The real Firefox run is the first CI run's**|
| V01 High verification question: activity expiry under a row lock that changes nothing| **A real defect, reproduced** against PostgreSQL 18.6: with a holder that only `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`s the row and the clock crossing the idle boundary (or the absolute end) while the write waits, the write returned `true` and extended the session; the control passed. PostgreSQL re-checks a waiting UPDATE only against a row that was *changed*, and the earlier tests changed it| `recordActivity` takes the row lock first (`SELECT 1 ... FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, which waits for every holder and, under READ COMMITTED, locks the latest version), and only then runs the statement that judges on the clock as it is after the wait. One row and no other lock, so the lock order of §13.10a is unchanged. The write waits at most the request's lock time (default 2 s); a longer wait is ended by the database and the recorder logs it| `session-store.db.test.ts`, "a lock that changes nothing, held while the clock crosses a boundary": the idle boundary, the absolute end and a control with the lock released early; the boundary is two seconds away and is crossed by polling the database's clock, with no long sleep and no guarded column changed. RED before, GREEN after, stable over three runs. The two older OB-131 tests in `activity.db.test.ts` only named the blocked statement and were updated to the new one|

**The ambiguity of F01, decided and recorded for the owner.** The specification puts the tier (step 6) before the scope (step 7), and what a *particular target* needs is a fact of its record, which the platform can read only for a caller who may see it. The choice made, which is the one that discloses nothing: a caller who cannot see the record (outside their hubs, or there is none) is answered by step 7 and never learns what the record needs; for a visible record the requirement is decided before validation. The consequence is that a caller below the tier a hidden record would need is told `HUB_SCOPE_VIOLATION` or `NOT_FOUND`, not `INSUFFICIENT_AUTHORITY`. The opposite order would let the answer to "may I?" disclose a fact about a record the caller may not see. Permission-enforcement.md §5.1 states the rule. **Confirmed by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026)).**

**Boundaries the review asked to keep explicit.** The ordering proved by `policy-change-races.db.test.ts` and `activity.db.test.ts` is a state-changing request against a **bundle reduction**. `OperationRunner.mutate` rereads the session under the shared policy locks and does not lock the session row; a direct revocation (a sign-out, an administrator's) takes identity and session row locks and no policy lock. It is not proof of every kind of revocation race, and the review does not establish that completing an already-authorized request is forbidden. **OB-132 (new):** the intended order of an already-authorized mutation against a direct revocation of its session is defined, and proved, by the operations that revoke it: `signOut` (S0.34) and the sessions administration (S0.37), on the canonical lock order (policy, identity, session). No blanket actor or session lock is added now: it could introduce a deadlock against the multi-principal plan.

**The review's recommended answers to the owner's pending decisions** (recommendations, not owner decisions; nothing is built on them beyond the wording named):

| Question| Recommendation| In this tree|
|---|---|---|
| The policy-change capability and functions (finding 1 of [§7.18](#718-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026))| Accept the narrow controlled post-seed path; the API runtime cannot execute the mutation functions; their callers are not extended for SLICE-009 implicitly| As built; approved by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026))|
| How SLICE-009 reaches policy edits (finding 2)| A named prerequisite of that slice: actor authorization, an audited change and atomic revocation, without migration credentials in the API| Recorded in finding 2; not built|
| `own` to and from `assigned` (finding 3)| Accept the conservative classification as a reduction: the two are not subsets of each other| As built; approved by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026))|
| The audit correlation id (finding 6)| A bounded syntax is validation and no screen for a secret or personal data; require an opaque identifier generated by the caller and never feed user text; align the SQL and application wording| The wording is corrected in the migration comment, the TypeScript door, SECURITY_DESIGN.md and MIGRATION_AND_SEEDING.md, and finding 6 above; approved by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026))|
| `libnss3-tools` in CI (finding 8)| Accept as the isolated Linux trust tooling; keep the trust stores inside the disposable run| As built; approved by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026))|
| The `integrated` job timeout, 25 to 30 minutes (finding 8)| Reasonable provisionally; record the actual job and step durations, because a larger deadline cures no hung process| As built; the 30 minutes are approved by the owner (12 October 2026, [§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026)); the durations are recorded when a hosted run exists|
| OB-100 (finding 13)| Accept the code-less `500` only for a committed, stranded `IN_PROGRESS` row that violates the atomic begin, work, complete invariant; a duplicate that waits on the original transaction is not that; a wait that times out is not automatically that; no silent reclaim or retry under a fresh key; record the distinction and test it before the dependent acceptance item closes| Refined in finding 13 and tested: a duplicate whose wait on an uncommitted first command runs out of time is answered `503` with no code and reclaims nothing, the same key owns the command when the first ends, and a committed record left in progress is the separate `500` placeholder. Approved by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026)); the wording that this makes stale is listed there, and the dependent acceptance item is closed by the task that owns it|

**The review's advice on the cost of a batch, and what this pass did with it.** Related tasks stay in bounded batches with a checkpoint for each (no universal one-task rule); `lint` and the affected `typecheck` run early, then focused behavioural tests; a failing regression comes before a fix, and the 90 deliberate breaks of the first pass were more than the agreed cadence needed (this pass made four breaks, of the new code, only to show its tests discriminate); one appropriate gate after production changes settle, and only the document guards after a documentation-only edit; the lower-concurrency local profile (`test:local`) stays explicit and is no diagnosis of the Ops Portal worker-start failures; database setup is profiled before a template database is adopted (not adopted: the aggregate of seven minutes does not say where the time goes, and the optimisation is deferred from this pass); test resource ownership is a harness concern, and the leftover databases that broke a role test were a defect of that test: it dropped a cluster-wide role, which PostgreSQL refuses while any other database holds a privilege of it, and it now **renames** the role inside the transaction it rolls back, which checks the same thing (the catalogue finds no role of that name) and depends on no other database (shown with a throwaway database of its own that held a privilege of the role: the drop form failed and the rename form passed; the throwaway was dropped; no permission to delete was widened, and the cause of the leftovers, runs killed by their own timeouts, is not removed); and only the plan's affected rows are updated, with no handoff file.

**Gate of the corrected tree** (uncached, `TURBO_FORCE=true`, Node 24.21.0, pnpm 11.1.3, Windows 11, a local PostgreSQL 18.6; run once on the settled tree, from 16:00 to 16:17 UTC: `138d02c` plus 137 changed paths, 81 modified and 56 new, unstaged and unchanged from the start of the gate to its end). It is the affected gate: the production changes are in the API (authorization, sessions) and the e2e harness. **Every command exited 0:** `format:check` (17 s), `lint` (79 s), `typecheck` (23 s), `build` (11 s), `contract:check` (0 operations compared: the machinery only) and `db:check`; `test:local` (270 s, five of five tasks, none cached): root 1205 tests (1203 passed, 2 skipped), API 170 files (5224 passed, 8 skipped), Ops Portal 23 files (220 passed), api-client 9 files (250 passed), harness 20 files (407 passed, 3 skipped); `test:db` (483 s): **51 files, 1495 passed, 2 skipped, 1 todo**; `test:e2e`: 20 passed (Chromium over plain HTTP; 94 s with its build). Against the first gate of [§7.18](#718-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026) the suites grew by 28 tests in the API, 17 in `test:db` and 41 in the harness.

- **The catalogue test that failed the first gate passed with the leftovers present.** The gate ran with ten marked `melarc_test_*` databases on the server, six of which hold privileges of the capability role (the very condition that made `catalog-invariants.db.test.ts` fail in the first gate), and `catalog-invariants.db.test.ts` passed (54 tests): the rename form depends on no other database.
- **Not rerun, and why:** `api-client:check` (no contract or client change), `test:browser` (the Ops Portal and its tests are unchanged), `pnpm audit` (no dependency changed). **Not run:** `test:e2e:https` and every proof that needs a test CA, `certutil` or an engine's trust store (so the strict negative test, the probe, the per-test Firefox profile and the isolation spec have run only as units), WebKit's mechanism, the branded Chrome and Edge smoke, the canonical `pnpm test` at default concurrency, every Linux, WSL2 and CI check, including a hosted run of this tree (nothing is pushed). `melarc_dev` was not changed.
- **The first launch of this gate was lost, and none of its logs is used.** I stopped it mid-run to add the catalogue fix; stopping the background task did not end its script, which ran on beside the second launch for about four minutes, wrote to the same log directory, and started a `test:db` that I then killed. That killed run left six more marked test databases (`melarc_test_239e4876`, `_25bf58e9`, `_2d5a3475`, `_50edbcff`, `_8a58f6d5`, `_d092fac1`) and, it seems, two with no marker (`melarc_test_75aca5d6`, `_a32692b8`). Both launches were stopped, no process of either was left, and the gate above is the third start, in a directory of its own. The owner's authorization of 12 October covered five other databases, so **none of these was dropped**: with the four marked leftovers of earlier runs (`_0ad977c7`, `_36dad696`, `_53fec8b3`, `_7500ca4d`) and the older unmarked `melarc_test_unmarked12c5bfb2` they are the owner's to remove, and with the catalogue test fixed they no longer fail anything. `pnpm --filter @melarc/api run db:orphans` lists the marked ones. *(The owner then authorized the removal of the ten marked ones, and they were removed; the three unmarked ones were kept: [§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026).)*
- **Skipped, not passed:** as in [§7.18](#718-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026) (API 8, `test:db` 2, root 2) and the harness's 3 (the generator's POSIX-mode test, one Linux-only test of the managed-process harness, and the POSIX-mode check of the profile copy, which is skipped on Windows); each needs Linux, WSL2 or CI.

**Gate 3 status after the review.** Not passed and not claimed. The review's four findings and its question are corrected and tested as far as this machine reaches (the database, the HTTP pipeline and the harness units); what it reaches only through a browser is unrun: the strict negative test, the endpoint probe, the per-test Firefox profile and its isolation spec, and every other proof of S0.23. What stands in the way is unchanged: S0.23's first run in a clean environment (`pnpm run test:e2e:https`, in CI or on a machine the owner clears for key material), S0.28 (the owner's evidence), the owner's decisions (findings 1 to 3, 6, 8 and 13 of [§7.18](#718-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026), and the ambiguity of F01 above; approved on 12 October 2026 except finding 2: [§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026)) and a hosted Linux run of the final revision. Verdict for the corrected tree: **local implementation verified, with pending evidence.**

**The second review, of the corrected tree (12 October 2026), and its follow-up.** A second independent review of the corrected package (`melarc-gate-3-corrected-source.zip`, SHA-256 `4630ae91bdb12f5e0f910795567bb69d38028a2905648cf7321de69bfcf453b1`, 881 files) verified the archive's hash and the hash of every source file, read the changes against the previous archive, and ran isolated checks of its own on the classifier (F03) and on the profile copies (F04). It **did not rerun PostgreSQL or the application suites**. It found V01, F01, F02, F03 and F04 corrected (F03 and F04 only as far as units reach: no browser evidence exists yet), judged the tree **ready for a checkpoint commit and a push for CI, with Gate 3 still pending**, and advised against another broad correction campaign. It asked for one focused follow-up, **done**: `withFreshProfile` caught a failure of `context.close()` and said nothing, so a test that passed was reported as passing with its context not closed (the review reproduced it; four new tests were RED here first). The teardown now runs whatever the test did, the copy is removed even when the context will not close, and nothing is swallowed (`ProfileTeardownError`, in the F04 row above). Changed after the gate of the corrected tree: `e2e/harness/fresh-profile.ts` and its test, and this plan. Run on that change: `fresh-profile.test.ts` (14 passed, 1 skipped on Windows), the harness's unit suite (20 files, 410 passed, 3 skipped), `lint`, the e2e `typecheck`, and `prettier` on the two files. Nothing else was repeated, because nothing else changed; the gate above stands for the rest of the tree. The status above stands.

**The second review's recommended decisions** (recommendations, not owner decisions; nothing is built on them beyond the wording named). Its advice to keep the narrow policy-change authority, the conservative `own` and `assigned` reduction, generated correlation identifiers, the isolated CI trust tooling and the limits of OB-100 repeats rows of the table above and is not repeated. The rest:

| Question| Recommendation| In this tree|
|---|---|---|
| Tier precedence (the ambiguity of F01 above)| Approve it and record the approval explicitly, because it clarifies the specification: an operation-wide minimum is checked without inspecting a target; a target-specific requirement is disclosed only after visibility is established; both come before body validation| Built, and stated in permission-enforcement.md §5.1; approved by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026))|
| The catalogue test that renames the role in place of dropping it| Accept: it tests the absence of the role name without unrelated cross-database grants blocking `DROP ROLE`; it does not resolve the separate cleanup of leftover databases| Built; approved by the owner on 12 October 2026 ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026))|
| Leftover test databases| **No blanket authorization.** An exact inventory of names, ownership markers and live sessions, then approval of only the individually verified disposable ones; a database with no marker is left alone| The inventory is below; the owner then authorized exactly the ten marked test databases, which were removed, and the rest kept ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026))|

**Inventory of the databases named `melarc*` on the local server** (read-only, taken after the follow-up; no session on any of them). `melarc_dev`: owner `melarc_owner`, marker `melarc:disposable`, the configured development database with migration `0000` only (**not a leftover and not to be touched**). Ten test databases owned by `melarc_owner` and marked `melarc:disposable`: **six that hold privileges of the capability role**, left by the `test:db` that I killed in the orphaned first launch of the gate (`melarc_test_239e4876`, `_25bf58e9`, `_2d5a3475`, `_50edbcff`, `_8a58f6d5`, `_d092fac1`), and **four that do not** (`_0ad977c7`, `_36dad696`, `_53fec8b3`, `_7500ca4d`), leftovers of earlier runs that cannot be attributed one by one. Three databases owned by `postgres` that carry **no marker** (`melarc_test_75aca5d6`, `_a32692b8`, `_unmarked12c5bfb2`): `db:orphans` neither lists nor drops them, and they are left alone. Dropping any of the ten marked ones is destructive and needs the owner's authorization, database by database. *(That authorization was then given for exactly these ten, and they were removed: [§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026).)*

### 7.20 The owner's approvals of 12 October 2026

**What this is.** After the second review of [§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition), the owner (1) approved the decisions below, to be recorded as theirs; (2) authorized the checkpoint commit of the reviewed tree, staged by an explicit path list and attributed only accurately, its push to `origin/feat/slice-000-gate-3`, and the use of the existing GitHub Actions workflow to obtain the hosted Linux and `test:e2e:https` evidence for the exact pushed revision (a draft pull request against `main` only if one is needed to trigger the checks; no merge and no deployment; a failing step is diagnosed from its log, with no assertion weakened, no certificate validation bypassed, no engine silently skipped, no timeout raised without evidence and no repeated rerun of an unchanged failure); and (3) authorized the removal of exactly ten named test databases (below). Gate 3 stays **pending** until its required proofs pass, S0.28 stays separately pending, and no next implementation stage was begun. This section records the approvals and the cleanup; it does not state the result of the push or of the hosted run, which a commit cannot contain.

| #| The owner approved| It applies to| State|
|---|---|---|---|
| 1| The refined tier precedence: the operation-wide minimum tier first; a target-sensitive tier only after visibility is established, before validation, with authoritative revalidation in the acting transaction| The ambiguity of F01 and F01 in [§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition); permission-enforcement.md §5.1; `OperationRunner.requireTargetTier`| Approved; as built|
| 2| The transactional catalogue-test role rename in place of `DROP ROLE`| `catalog-invariants.db.test.ts` ([§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition), the gate)| Approved; as built|
| 3| The narrow policy-change authority (finding 1); the conservative `own` and `assigned` reduction classification (finding 3); generated correlation identifiers (finding 6); the isolated CI trust tooling (`libnss3-tools`) and the proposed 30-minute `integrated` job timeout (finding 8)| [§7.18](#718-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026) findings 1, 3, 6 and 8; migration `0017`; `ci.yml`| Approved; as built. **Not covered, and still open:** finding 2 (how SLICE-009's edit reaches the functions) and finding 4 (a bundle's audience and its `privileged` flag), which stay with the slice that needs them|
| 4| OB-100's code-less `500` **only** for a committed, stranded `IN_PROGRESS` invariant failure; ordinary concurrent execution and bounded lock-wait failures remain distinct; **no automatic reclamation**| [§7.18](#718-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026) finding 13; SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.16; `refusal-order.db.test.ts`| Approved as the answer. No contract code is added, and the retention-cleanup reading of "who reclaims" in finding 13 (2) is **not** adopted; OB-113 and OB-114 are otherwise unchanged. As built, a stranded record answers every request under its key with that `500` and changes nothing, until a person resolves it. **Wording owed, not changed in the checkpoint:** SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.16 still calls the answer "not decided" and the reclaiming "undecided"; the comment, the title ("blocked") and the `it.todo` of the OB-100 test still describe it as pending; DEPLOYMENT_AND_ENVIRONMENTS.md (S0.20 "does not do") still says the answer is "the owner's to decide". They are brought into line by the task that closes the dependent acceptance item, or earlier on the owner's instruction|

**The database cleanup.** The owner first authorized no deletion in the task of the checkpoint and then, in a later instruction, the removal of exactly the ten marked test databases of the inventory in [§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition), each to be rechecked before it was dropped, none by wildcard and none by ending anyone's session. *Rechecked, read-only, immediately before:* for each name, exactly one database of that exact name, its comment exactly `melarc:disposable`, owner `melarc_owner` and no session; and no test, gate, database-tool or API process running on the machine. All ten passed. *Removed* with `db:orphans --drop` naming the ten (no pattern and no "all", and without `--disconnect`: a plain `DROP DATABASE`, which the server refuses while any session is connected; none was refused): `melarc_test_0ad977c7`, `melarc_test_239e4876`, `melarc_test_25bf58e9`, `melarc_test_2d5a3475`, `melarc_test_36dad696`, `melarc_test_50edbcff`, `melarc_test_53fec8b3`, `melarc_test_7500ca4d`, `melarc_test_8a58f6d5`, `melarc_test_d092fac1`. *Retained, as the owner required:* `melarc_dev` (the development database, migration `0000` only) and the three unmarked databases `melarc_test_75aca5d6`, `melarc_test_a32692b8` and `melarc_test_unmarked12c5bfb2`. A read-only inventory afterwards listed exactly those four, none with a session. No full test run was needed for the cleanup and none was made.

**The three unmarked databases, inspected read-only** (read-only transactions, rolled back; names and counts only: no row value was read and no credential printed). `melarc_test_75aca5d6` and `melarc_test_a32692b8` are **empty**: the `public` schema only, no table, function or type, only the `plpgsql` extension; owner `postgres`, no comment, no explicit access list, no role holds anything in them, 7.4 MB, no session. `melarc_test_unmarked12c5bfb2` is the same with **one table, `public.precious`, holding one row**. *Likely origin:* negative-case fixtures that tests create on purpose through the administrator's connection with a bare `CREATE DATABASE`, which is why they carry no marker and belong to `postgres`, to prove that the tooling refuses a database it did not make. `reset-tools.db.test.ts` makes `melarc_test_unmarked<hex>` with `create table precious (id integer)` and one inserted row and checks that `db:reset` refuses it and leaves the row: `melarc_test_unmarked12c5bfb2` is that fixture. `orphans.db.test.ts` and `e2e-database.db.test.ts` each create a bare `melarc_test_<8 hex>` the same way, which is what the other two are (the databases do not say which of the two made which). Each of those tests records the name before creating the database and force-drops it in its `afterAll`, so a leftover means that the run ended before `afterAll` ran, or that the drop failed. **Not deleted**: the owner asked that they be inspected first; `db:orphans` cannot remove them (it refuses a database with no marker, by design), so removing them takes an administrator's `DROP DATABASE` by name, on the owner's authorization.

**OB-133 (new): a test database made by a run that fails, is killed or is cancelled stays on the server.** *The defect.* A database test makes its database and removes it in its own `afterAll` (`createTestDatabase().drop()`; the deliberately unmarked fixtures are force-dropped by name). When the run ends before `afterAll` runs (a process killed or cancelled, a worker ended by a timeout, a gate script stopped from outside; stopping the background task that ran a gate did not end its script: [§7.19](#719-independent-review-of-the-gate-3-completion-batch-12-october-2026-disposition)) or the drop itself fails, nothing removes the database. *Evidence on this machine:* thirteen leftovers beside `melarc_dev` on 12 October 2026, ten marked and three unmarked, from runs of this and of earlier batches; six of the marked ones were made by one run of mine that I killed. *Effects:* (a) they accumulate (7 to 9 MB each); (b) a leftover that holds a privilege of the cluster-wide capability role makes any test that drops that role fail, which broke `catalog-invariants.db.test.ts` in the first gate: that test no longer depends on other databases (the rename approved above), which cures the symptom and not the cause; (c) the marker is a constant string, `melarc:disposable`, with no run, process or time in it, so neither the tool nor a person can tell an abandoned database from a run's that is between steps except by what they know; (d) the unmarked fixtures cannot be removed with the supported tool at all. A GitHub-hosted runner is a fresh machine for each run, so this concerns a developer machine. *What exists and stays:* `db:orphans` lists the marked databases and removes only those a person names, one by one, never by idleness, age or pattern (audit F07: no run removes another run's database because it looks idle). *Not built, by the owner's instruction:* any broad or automatic sweeper. *Narrow options for the owner to choose from later, none built, each keeping removal by name and by a person:* record the creating run and process (and the time) in each database's comment, so that a listing can say "made by a process that is gone"; give the deliberate negative fixtures a marker of their own that `db:orphans` lists and removes by name; a start-of-run check that only reports what earlier runs left. *Until then:* whoever stops a run lists with `db:orphans` and removes, by name, only what they know is theirs, and a gate is stopped by ending its whole process tree and not by stopping the task that launched it.

### 7.21 The first hosted run of the checkpoint (12 October 2026), its diagnosis and the correction

**What this is.** The owner pushed the checkpoint `319c1c3` ([§7.20](#720-the-owners-approvals-of-12-october-2026)) to `origin/feat/slice-000-gate-3` and opened [pull request #4](https://github.com/melarclogistics/Melarc/pull/4) against `main` (not a draft). This section records what the first hosted run showed, the owner's decision and the correction. It states the result of **that** run, which the commit made before it could not.

**The run.** [Run 38284306211](https://github.com/melarclogistics/Melarc/actions/runs/38284306211), event `pull_request`, attempt 1, 17:27:17Z to 17:32:14Z. **Tested revision:** GitHub's merge commit `761adfe0b3301d1cea4110ee90ef789be4609418` (parents `33bc158`, the tip of `main`, and `319c1c3`, the head of the pull request); its tree `fac8c66fc4498a6f506fcdd8460da8db523dba2c` is identical to the tree of `319c1c3` (checked by fetching `refs/pull/4/merge`). Jobs: Static checks success (1 min 28 s); Unit, component and process tests success (2 min 42 s); Database tests success (4 min 10 s); Dependency audit success (32 s); Build, contract and browser tests (`integrated`) **failure** (4 min 37 s) at step 11 only, "Browser, API and database journey over the trusted HTTPS origin" (`pnpm run test:e2e:https`, exit 1, 53 s); CI result failure, as the aggregate of that one job. Steps 1 to 10 of `integrated` succeeded: the build (6 s), `contract:check` (2 s), the browsers (52 s), `test:browser` over three engines (83 s), the plain journey (44 s) and the NSS tools. The diagnostics artifact `melarc-diagnostics-761adfe0b3301d1cea4110ee90ef789be4609418-1` (201,666 bytes) was kept. **This is the first hosted Linux run of this tree**: the static, unit and database jobs passed, which include the tests Windows skips. The durations above are the ones the 30-minute budget of the `integrated` job was to be judged by (a failed run: a passing HTTPS suite takes longer).

**What the log showed.** The step's log is readable only to a signed-in account (the session reads the public job, step and annotation data; the log returned 403 and the artifact 401), so the owner supplied screenshots. Playwright's summary: 22 passed, 1 did not run, and a list of failures of which the visible part is nine WebKit tests. With 11 tests an engine (33 in all) that leaves **ten failures: nine visible, and one above the screenshot, in Chromium or Firefox, not yet identified**. The nine are every WebKit test that needs the CA trusted (the ordinary project's headers, isolation and trust tests; the secret project's four cookie tests and its origin test); the second isolation test did not run (serial mode, after the first failed); WebKit's refusal, the negative control, is not among them. So the generator, `certutil`, the per-engine stores and the TLS front worked well enough for 22 tests to pass.

**The diagnosis: WebKit ignores `SSL_CERT_FILE`.** The inference from the numbers is backed by four checks. The pinned WebKit bundle (revision 2359, the WPE build, read as inert data) carries no TLS library of its own, its wrapper sets only `WEBKIT_*` and `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, and it requires the system `ca-certificates` package. glib-networking's GnuTLS backend fills its trust list from GnuTLS's system trust (`gnutls_x509_trust_list_add_system_trust`); only its OpenSSL backend calls `X509_STORE_set_default_paths`, which would honour `SSL_CERT_FILE`, and Ubuntu 24.04's `glib-networking` package ships `libgiognutls.so` and no OpenSSL module. GnuTLS 3.8.3 (the version of Ubuntu 24.04) reads no environment variable for its trust store. **The exact WebKit error text was not seen**: the diagnosis rests on this evidence and on the pattern of the failures, and the next run confirms or refutes it.

**The owner's decision (A), 12 October 2026.** Put to the owner: (A) on a GitHub-hosted runner only, put the name-constrained disposable CA in the system store for WebKit's proofs and remove it when they end; (B) declare WebKit's HTTPS proofs blocked on Linux, visibly, with Chromium and Firefox mandatory. `ignoreHTTPSErrors` was excluded by the owner's rules. The owner chose A. For WebKit alone this supersedes the statement of finding 8 that no trust store of the runner is changed, and the rule "never trust a CA machine-wide" of `e2e/CLAUDE.md` has this one exception.

**The correction**, test first (each new test was seen to fail for its stated reason before the code existed): `harness/system-trust.ts` (new, and the only code that touches a system store: allowed only on Linux with `CI` and `GITHUB_ACTIONS` equal to `true` and `RUNNER_ENVIRONMENT` equal to `github-hosted`, never on a machine declared cleared for key material; `sudo -n install -m 0644 <ca> /usr/local/share/ca-certificates/melarc-e2e-disposable-ca.crt` and `update-ca-certificates`; removal by `rm -f` and `update-ca-certificates --fresh`, both tried whatever the first does and every failure said; an absolute path only); `harness/engine-trust.ts` (WebKit's plan names no environment value, has `scope: 'system'`, and is unsupported with the reason on any machine that is not a GitHub-hosted runner); `harness/https-run.ts` (**two runs**: Chromium, Firefox and WebKit's refusal while the system store lacks the CA, then WebKit's other proofs with the CA in it; both run whatever the first did; the CA is taken out straight after and again in the `finally`, before the directory goes; a CA that cannot be taken out fails the run; the first failure is the exit status; where the store may not be changed the second run is made without it, says so, and WebKit's proofs fail to trust); `playwright.config.ts` and `tests/https-trust.spec.ts` (the refusal is tagged `@untrusted-control` and run alone by the new project `https-webkit-control`, which the other WebKit project excludes: the control is honest only while the CA is not in the system store, refused before and trusted after with nothing else differing; a test holds the partition, and `playwright test --list` shows 1, 5 and 5 tests for WebKit's three projects); and `turbo.json`, **a trap the run would have met**: Turborepo's strict environment passes only the variables listed, `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `RUNNER_ENVIRONMENT` were not, so the run would have seen `CI` alone and refused to touch the system store; both are listed and `scripts/turbo-config.test.ts` holds them. The documents changed with it: `e2e/README.md`, `e2e/CLAUDE.md`, `DEVELOPMENT.md`, the comments of `ci.yml` and of the harness.

**Evidence of the correction**, from a machine with no Linux, `sudo`, WebKit or CA: the harness's unit suite (21 files, 452 passed and 3 skipped, 42 more than before); the root guards of `turbo-config`, `test-inventory` and `ci-workflow` (370 passed); `playwright test --list` for the projects; `eslint`, the e2e `tsc` and Prettier. **Not run, because it cannot be run here:** the real `sudo` and `update-ca-certificates` path, GnuTLS reading the bundle with a name-constrained CA, WebKit trusting it, and whatever the tenth failure is. The hosted run of the correction is the proof.

**What is still open.** The tenth failure, a Chromium or Firefox test above the screenshot, is unidentified; if the next run fails there, its log is needed. WebKit's trust through the system store is unproven until that run. S0.23 stays blocked until a hosted run passes in full, Gate 3 stays pending, and S0.28 is the owner's, separately. The correction is a commit on top of `319c1c3` that carries this section; whether it is pushed, and the result of its hosted run, are not stated here. The pull request is not a draft, which the owner may change.

**The second hosted run (13 October 2026), and a flake it exposed.** [Run 38390504553](https://github.com/melarclogistics/Melarc/actions/runs/38390504553), event `pull_request`, started 11:28:56Z, tested `319c1c3` again: the correction (`52d06f9`) had not been pushed yet. The HTTPS suite therefore failed exactly as before (22 passed, 1 did not run, the same WebKit tests, 52 s), which shows that failure is deterministic and not a flake of the run. The Database tests job, which passed in the first run, **failed one test**: `guard-chain.db.test.ts`, "stores a keyed digest and no identifier" (1496 passed, 1 failed, 1 todo). *The reason, from the log:* the test asserted that the text of each row of the limiter's table does not contain "ada", and the row held a random keyed digest whose hexadecimal text contains "ada" (`…695adae98d…`) and no identifier. It is a defect of the test and not of the product: a word made of the letters a to f occurs in a random 64-character digest about once in 60 rows, so the job fails by chance in some runs of every hundred. *Fixed test first:* `src/test-support/clear-identifier.ts` looks for an identifier as a whole word (no letter or digit before or after it), and its test uses the row of the failing run (it contains "ada" and holds no identifier), the places where a hexadecimal run can hold the letters, and the ways a stored identifier shows (in a column of its own, quoted, in a key, joined with an underscore, in another case, in an address); the database test uses it and also checks the identifier it signs in with. A search of the repository for the same pattern (an absence assertion on a short word made only of hexadecimal letters) found no other. The file's five tests pass against the local PostgreSQL; the full `test:db` was not repeated for this change.

## 9. Stop conditions and the task handoff

**Stop conditions** are those of the [runbook](BOOTSTRAP_B0_RUNBOOK.md#7-stop-conditions), and also stop the task when: a decision contradicts a retained document it does not override; a product value a task needs is unset (surface it, never default it); a change would break a stable identifier; `contract:check` or `api-client:check` fails for a reason not traceable to the task's own edit; a step needs a machine trust-store or global tool change; the task would touch unrelated work; a port cannot be placed without a new domain; a deadlock appears in a concurrency run (the lock order is then a specification defect for the owner); a package or tool is not already approved.

**Handoff.** End each task in the runbook's [format](BOOTSTRAP_B0_RUNBOOK.md#8-task-handoff-format), extended for this slice:

```text
Task: S0.NN, title
Git: HEAD, branch, and the scope of uncommitted change
Files changed:
Decisions implemented (document and section):
Commands run (exact) and exit codes:
Result: counts; every skipped test with its reason and the environment it needs
Evidence reached, per criterion: fixture/platform verified, or real-operation verified
Obligations closed, and obligations carried (OB-nn)
Checks not run, and why (services, browser, platform)
Problem or blocker, if any:
Findings the Product Owner must decide: conflicts, provisional values, suggestions
```

Raw logs are kept only when they help debugging or CI.
